| SEM-983 | Rejected interpretation | `Delta(Delta-1)^(i-1)` predicts the expected BFS frontier. | It is a collision-free capacity ceiling only. | Note 119 |
| SEM-984 | Rejected equivalence | Moore layer deficit equals the number of duplicate candidates. | Low degree, same-layer edges, back edges, convergence, and closure all reduce new states differently. | Note 119 |
| SEM-985 | Scope boundary | One root with eccentricity at most `D` suffices for its ball count, while diameter at most `D` supplies this condition for every root. | Connected component declared. | Note 119 |
| SEM-986 | Proved defect identity | `M(Delta,D)-|V|` equals the sum of per-layer capacity deficits from any root of eccentricity at most `D`. | Capacities use the same `Delta,D`. | Layer partition; note 119 |
| SEM-987 | Symmetry fact | In a vertex-transitive graph, BFS sphere sizes and Moore deficit profiles are root-independent. | Fixed graph metric. | Automorphism argument; note 119 |
| SEM-988 | Scope boundary | Degree-diameter and degree-girth Moore bounds can share a formula while giving opposite order inequalities. | Odd-girth vertex-rooted case versus diameter case. | Notes 27 and 119 |
| SEM-989 | Qualified fact | Equality in the undirected degree-diameter Moore bound forces regular collision-free layer capacity and Moore-graph rigidity. | Finite connected simple graph. | Miller-Siran; note 119 |
| SEM-990 | Directed fact | A digraph of maximum out-degree `d` and directed diameter at most `D` has order at most `1+d+...+d^D`. | Directed reachability convention declared. | Directed BFS tree count; note 119 |
| SEM-991 | Rejected transfer | The undirected `Delta-1` formula applies unchanged to a positive directed move alphabet. | An outgoing traversal need not consume an inverse outgoing arc. | Note 119 |
| SEM-992 | Cayley fact | A finite Cayley graph's group order is bounded by the Moore capacity for its true simple degree and diameter. | Connected finite Cayley graph. | Note 119 |
| SEM-993 | Evidence boundary | Generator-label count need not equal simple graph degree. | Identity, duplicate, inverse, and parallel-label conventions declared. | Notes 17, 27, and 119 |
| SEM-994 | Rejected implication | The unrestricted Moore ceiling proves that a Cayley or Schreier graph near that order exists. | Algebraic symmetry is an additional restriction. | Dougherty-Faber; note 119 |
| SEM-995 | Reporting rule | Moore capacity, realized frontiers, duplicate work, state bytes, owner balance, communication, and measured GPU throughput are distinct claims. | Degree/depth and graph convention declared. | Note 119 |
| SEM-996 | Proved fact | A source BFS histogram satisfies `T(s)=sum_i i*|F_i(s)|`, the source farness or transmission. | Finite connected unweighted graph and exhaustive BFS. | Layer partition; note 120 |
| SEM-997 | Proved generating-function fact | For `P_s(z)=sum_i |F_i(s)|z^i`, `P_s(1)=n` and `P_s'(1)=T(s)`. | Complete source histogram. | Formal differentiation; note 120 |
| SEM-998 | Rejected equivalence | Equal eccentricity implies equal farness or closeness. | Triangle-with-leaf vertices `b,d` both have eccentricity two but transmissions four and five. | Note 120 |
| SEM-999 | Proved fact | The undirected Wiener index equals one half the sum of all vertex transmissions. | Finite connected undirected graph. | Ordered-pair double counting; note 120 |
| SEM-1000 | Scope boundary | One BFS gives one distance-matrix row sum, not the Wiener index of a general graph. | No transitivity or other structural shortcut assumed. | Note 120 |
| SEM-1001 | Definition | Harmonic centrality is `sum_(v!=s)1/d(s,v)` with reciprocal infinity zero, equivalently `sum_(i>=1)|F_i|/i`. | Directed orientation and normalization declared. | Boldi-Vigna; note 120 |
| SEM-1002 | Rejected equivalence | Harmonic centrality and reciprocal-farness closeness are the same aggregation on connected graphs. | Sum of reciprocals differs from reciprocal of sum. | Note 120 |
| SEM-1003 | Directed boundary | Outgoing and incoming distance centralities require forward and reverse reachability respectively. | Directed graph. | Note 120 |
| SEM-1004 | Cayley symmetry fact | Every root of a finite connected Cayley graph has the same distance histogram and transmission. | Fixed generator metric and valid translation automorphisms. | Note 120 |
| SEM-1005 | Proved Cayley consequence | One identity-rooted complete BFS gives `W=n*T(e)/2` and mean pair distance `T(e)/(n-1)`. | Finite connected undirected Cayley graph with `n>1`. | Vertex transitivity and pair double counting; note 120 |
| SEM-1006 | Rejected transfer | A Schreier graph automatically inherits Cayley root-independent distance profiles. | The state action need not act by automorphisms of the fixed generator graph. | Note 120 |
| SEM-1007 | Evidence boundary | A radius-truncated weighted layer sum is a lower bound on full farness, not the full value before exhaustion. | Nonnegative distances and incomplete component. | Note 120 |
| SEM-1008 | Scope boundary | Sampled-source mean distance is an estimate, not an exact Wiener certificate without exhaustive or structural evidence. | Sampling procedure declared. | Note 120 |
| SEM-1009 | Arithmetic boundary | Distance sums should be accumulated exactly before declared normalization or reciprocal conversion. | Accumulator range covers the bound. | Note 120 |
| SEM-1010 | Reporting rule | Traversal, layer reduction, all-source or symmetry scope, communication, validation, and exact versus sampled distance aggregates are distinct claims. | Graph/source epoch declared. | Note 120 |
| SEM-1011 | Definition | Raw vertex betweenness sums `sigma_st(v)/sigma_st` over declared endpoint pairs with `v` internal. | Pair orientation, endpoint, path identity, and normalization declared. | Freeman; Brandes; note 121 |
| SEM-1012 | Rejected equivalence | Betweenness from one deterministic BFS parent tree equals shortest-path betweenness. | Diamond alternatives must split fractional contribution. | Note 121 |
| SEM-1013 | Proved recurrence | Source dependencies satisfy the reverse-depth Brandes recurrence using predecessor ratios `sigma_s(v)/sigma_s(w)`. | Complete source shortest-path DAG and valid arithmetic. | Brandes; note 121 |
| SEM-1014 | Ordering boundary | Source dependency cannot be finalized before every deeper shortest-path successor contribution is complete. | Reverse-depth DAG accumulation. | Note 121 |
| SEM-1015 | Proved global identity | Raw unordered internal-vertex betweenness sums to `W(G)-binom(n,2)`. | Finite connected undirected graph and fractional shortest-path convention. | Pairwise internal-length double counting; note 121 |
| SEM-1016 | Proved edge identity | Raw unordered edge betweenness sums to `W(G)`. | Matching edge/path identity and fractional convention. | Pairwise path-length double counting; note 121 |
| SEM-1017 | Rejected equivalence | Edge betweenness and the edge-Wiener index are the same invariant. | One counts shortest vertex-pair flow through edges; the other sums distances between edges. | Note 121 |
| SEM-1018 | Cayley symmetry fact | Every vertex of a finite connected Cayley graph has equal automorphism-invariant betweenness. | Fixed graph and betweenness convention. | Translation automorphisms; note 121 |
| SEM-1019 | Proved Cayley consequence | Raw unordered vertex betweenness equals `(T(e)-n+1)/2` at every vertex. | Finite connected undirected Cayley graph; endpoints excluded. | SEM-1015 plus note 120 transmission identity; note 121 |
| SEM-1020 | Rejected implication | Equal Cayley vertex betweenness means the common score is zero. | Vertex transitivity removes distinguished vertices but does not remove internal geodesics. | Cycles; note 121 |
| SEM-1021 | Rejected implication | Vertex-transitive Cayley graphs are automatically edge-transitive. | Generator-edge orbits may differ. | Note 121 |
| SEM-1022 | Qualified edge fact | In an edge-transitive graph, each raw unordered edge betweenness equals `W/|E|`. | Finite connected graph and matching convention. | SEM-1016 and edge transitivity; note 121 |
| SEM-1023 | Schreier boundary | Cayley equal-betweenness reduction does not transfer to a Schreier graph without automorphisms preserving the fixed generator graph. | Action and graph automorphisms distinguished. | Notes 120 and 121 |
| SEM-1024 | Arithmetic boundary | Overflowed path counts or incomplete reverse reductions can corrupt betweenness despite correct BFS distances. | Exact or explicitly bounded arithmetic and completed DAG phases required. | Notes 11, 30, and 121 |
| SEM-1025 | Reporting rule | BFS traversal, predecessor/count storage, reverse dependency, cross-owner reduction, source sampling or symmetry, and validation are distinct claims. | Source/graph epoch and normalization declared. | Note 121 |
| SEM-1026 | Definition | Ordering bandwidth is the maximum numeric span of an original graph edge under a vertex bijection. | Symmetric sparsity graph and simultaneous row/column order. | Cuthill-McKee; note 122 |
| SEM-1027 | Scope boundary | Matrix profile is a summed directional envelope objective, not the same as maximum bandwidth. | Profile convention declared. | Note 122 |
| SEM-1028 | Qualified fact | Cuthill-McKee is BFS with undiscovered neighbors queued by nondecreasing degree and a declared tie-break. | Undirected graph and chosen component root. | Cuthill-McKee; note 122 |
| SEM-1029 | Proved layer bound | Any contiguous BFS-layer ordering has bandwidth at most `max_i(|F_i|+|F_(i+1)|-1)`. | Finite simple undirected graph. | Edge depth-difference theorem; note 122 |
| SEM-1030 | Rejected implication | Exact BFS layers make the CM numbering globally bandwidth-optimal. | A star's layer-contiguous leaf-root order is worse than placing leaves around the center. | Note 122 |
| SEM-1031 | Proved reversal fact | Exact reversal of a vertex order preserves every edge span and therefore bandwidth. | `pi_R=n+1-pi`. | Direct algebra; note 122 |
| SEM-1032 | Rejected claim | RCM improves bandwidth relative to the corresponding CM order merely by reversing it. | Reversal preserves bandwidth; root/tie/preprocessing changes must explain any difference. | Note 122 |
| SEM-1033 | Qualified fact | RCM can change and often reduce profile because profile is directional. | Same symmetric pattern and declared profile convention. | Cuthill-Rose-Willoughby; note 122 |
| SEM-1034 | Evidence boundary | Pseudo-peripheral sweep termination does not generally certify a truly peripheral vertex or exact diameter. | Heuristic candidate policy. | George-Liu; notes 21 and 122 |
| SEM-1035 | Validation boundary | BFS correctness and ordering quality require separate checks. | Original pattern retained for recomputation. | Note 122 |
| SEM-1036 | Cayley fact | In a finite connected Cayley graph, regularity removes CM degree preference and vertex transitivity makes every root peripheral. | True simple Cayley graph. | Notes 21 and 122 |
| SEM-1037 | Rejected implication | Cayley regularity makes the CM ordering canonical. | Generator order and tie-breaking remain. | Note 122 |
| SEM-1038 | Invariance fact | Vertex reordering preserves graph metric invariants but can change storage locality, deterministic traversal order, and ID-based ownership. | Exact bijective permutation. | Note 122 |
| SEM-1039 | Implicit-graph boundary | A global CM order for an implicit puzzle graph may require complete enumeration and state remapping. | No preexisting order oracle assumed. | Note 122 |
| SEM-1040 | Reporting rule | Ordering construction, permutation, bandwidth/profile, ownership changes, BFS correctness, and measured hardware effects are distinct claims. | Representation and partition policy declared. | Note 122 |
| SEM-1041 | Proved metric fact | A covering projection cannot increase distance: `d_G(p(x),p(y))<=d_G_tilde(x,y)`. | Connected undirected graph cover. | Path projection; note 123 |
| SEM-1042 | Proved fiber-distance fact | Base distance from `s` to `t` equals minimum lifted distance from a fixed lift of `s` to the fiber over `t`. | Covering projection and connected graphs. | Unique lift of a base geodesic; note 123 |
| SEM-1043 | Proved ball fact | `p(B_r(x))=B_r(p(x))` for every radius under a graph cover. | Same cover and unweighted metric. | Projection and geodesic lifting; note 123 |
| SEM-1044 | Qualified layer fact | Every base depth-`i` vertex has a lift at depth `i`, but a lifted depth-`i` vertex may project to an earlier base layer. | Fixed lifted source. | Fiber minimization; note 123 |
| SEM-1045 | Definition | The universal cover tree has non-backtracking base walks as vertices and endpoint projection as its covering map. | Edge/dart identity declared. | Courcelle; Fiala-Paulusma; note 123 |
| SEM-1046 | Rejected equivalence | Universal-cover BFS depth is automatically base-state BFS distance. | A reduced history may end at a base state with a shorter history in another fiber representative. | Note 123 |
| SEM-1047 | Proved injectivity fact | If `2r<girth(G)`, the universal-cover projection is injective on the radius-`r` vertex ball. | Simple undirected base graph. | Closed-walk/cycle argument; notes 27 and 123 |
| SEM-1048 | Scope boundary | Vertex-ball injectivity does not imply induced-ball isomorphism; excluding extra projected edges needs `2r+1<girth`. | Simple undirected graph. | Odd-cycle boundary; notes 27 and 123 |
| SEM-1049 | Rejected fixed-lift claim | Every pair of fixed lifted vertices has the same distance as its projected pair. | `C6 -> C3` two-sheeted cover. | Note 123 |
| SEM-1050 | Rejected division rule | A base BFS layer size equals the corresponding cover-layer size divided by sheet count. | Fiber representatives can occur at different lifted depths. | Note 123 |
| SEM-1051 | Cayley fact | Under clean generator-edge conventions, a free-group Cayley tree covers the presented group's Cayley graph and fibers encode relations. | Distinct nonidentity generator images and local label bijection. | Note 123 |
| SEM-1052 | Schreier boundary | Universal-cover fiber collisions in a Schreier graph may come from stabilizer words rather than group identity relations. | Fixed action and base state. | Notes 16, 61, and 123 |
| SEM-1053 | Rejected implication | A group quotient or state canonicalization automatically induces a graph covering. | Generator images or neighbor edges may collapse, loop, or lose local bijection. | Notes 17 and 123 |
| SEM-1054 | Distributed boundary | History-key ownership does not implement base-state BFS until cross-owner fiber-equivalent endpoints are reconciled. | Base-state output contract. | Note 123 |
| SEM-1055 | Reporting rule | Lifted histories, projected candidates, unique base states, fiber collisions, reconciliation, injective radius, and base/cover throughput are distinct claims. | Cover and state identity declared. | Note 123 |
| SEM-1056 | Proved subgraph fact | Edge or vertex deletion cannot decrease distance between surviving vertices. | Same unit graph and endpoints; infinity allowed. | Path-set inclusion; notes 115, 118, and 124 |
| SEM-1057 | Proved supergraph fact | Adding edges cannot increase distance between old vertices. | Same endpoints and unit costs. | Path-set inclusion; note 124 |
| SEM-1058 | Proved contraction fact | Contracting an edge cannot increase distance between projected endpoints. | Unit undirected graph and quotient map. | Path projection; note 124 |
| SEM-1059 | Proved one-edge bound | One unit edge contraction decreases any fixed original-pair distance by at most one. | Both original endpoints mapped through the same single contraction. | Shortest contracted-path lift; note 124 |
| SEM-1060 | Proved cluster bound | A `k`-edge quotient path through clusters of diameter at most `Delta` lifts to length at most `k+(k+1)Delta`. | Connected clusters and retained cross-edge witnesses. | Concatenated intra-cluster paths; note 124 |
| SEM-1061 | Ball boundary | For a contraction quotient, `q(B_r^G(s))` is contained in the quotient ball, but reverse containment can fail at fixed radius. | Intra-cluster distance collapsed to zero. | Note 124 |
| SEM-1062 | Rejected implication | `H` being a minor of `G` determines one monotone inequality between their distances. | Deletions increase while contractions decrease distances. | `C4` deletion and path contraction; note 124 |
| SEM-1063 | Evidence boundary | A minor path is not replay-valid in the original graph without cross-edge and intra-branch-set witnesses. | Concrete endpoints and branch-set model declared. | Note 124 |
| SEM-1064 | Scope boundary | Simplifying loops and parallel edges after contraction preserves simple vertex distance but can change labels and shortest-path multiplicity. | Simple-distance output only. | Note 124 |
| SEM-1065 | Cayley quotient fact | Quotient-group word distance is no greater than original word distance and equals minimum original distance to the target coset. | Normal subgroup and compatible generator conventions. | Note 124 |
| SEM-1066 | Rejected fixed-target claim | A shortest quotient-group word automatically reaches one requested concrete representative. | It reaches a coset; a kernel correction may remain. | Note 124 |
| SEM-1067 | Cayley boundary | Generator images can become loops or collide in a quotient, changing label and multiplicity semantics. | Quotient generator map declared. | Note 124 |
| SEM-1068 | Exactness boundary | A contraction lower bound proves exact original distance only when it meets a replayable original upper bound. | Valid coarse inequality and original witness. | Note 124 |
| SEM-1069 | Distributed boundary | Contracting across owners changes state identity, ownership, and routing rather than merely storage layout. | Multi-GPU partition policy declared. | Note 124 |
| SEM-1070 | Reporting rule | Coarsening, coarse traversal, lifting, exactness gap, owner changes, and original traversal throughput are distinct claims. | Branch-set and graph epoch declared. | Note 124 |
| SEM-1071 | Proved subdivision fact | BFS distance between branch vertices in a positive-integer edge subdivision equals weighted shortest-path distance in the original graph. | Internally disjoint replacement path of length `ell(e)>=1` for every edge. | Path expansion and suppression; note 125 |
| SEM-1072 | Proved uniform fact | Uniform length-`k` subdivision multiplies every branch-to-branch distance by `k`. | `k>=1`; queries restricted to original branch vertices. | SEM-1071; note 125 |
| SEM-1073 | Proved layer fact | Under uniform subdivision, original sphere `S_j` is exactly the branch-vertex part of expanded sphere `S_(kj)`. | Branch source and uniform `k`. | Distance scaling; note 125 |
| SEM-1074 | Rejected implication | Nonuniform subdivision merely rescales ordinary BFS depth by one global constant. | Edge lengths may differ. | Lengths `1+1` versus direct length `3`; note 125 |
| SEM-1075 | Representation boundary | Zero or negative edge lengths are not representable by a positive unit-edge subdivision. | Literal unweighted path-length reduction. | Note 125 |
| SEM-1076 | Qualified path fact | Minimum weighted branch paths correspond to minimum unit paths in a proper subdivision. | Internally disjoint paths and original edge identity retained. | Expansion/suppression bijection; note 125 |
| SEM-1077 | Rejected preservation | Edge subdivision preserves full BFS frontier width and work. | New transit vertices participate in traversal. | Once-subdivided `K_n` has a `binom(n-1,2)` transit frontier; note 125 |
| SEM-1078 | Endpoint boundary | Uniform subdivision scales branch-restricted diameter and eccentricity, not automatically the all-vertex versions. | Query domain declared. | Transit vertices enlarge the domain; note 125 |
| SEM-1079 | Topological-minor boundary | Abstract pattern distance, subdivision-model length, and whole-host shortest distance are distinct. | Particular topological-minor model declared. | Note 125 |
| SEM-1080 | Rejected implication | A topological-minor model is automatically an isometric embedding in its host. | Host may provide external shortcuts and branch paths may have unequal lengths. | Note 125 |
| SEM-1081 | Directed boundary | Directed weighted arcs require orientation-preserving chains and may require separate interiors by direction. | Positive integer arc lengths. | Note 125 |
| SEM-1082 | Label boundary | Transit unit edges are phases of a macro-edge, not automatically original move labels. | Replayable labeled transition output. | Note 125 |
| SEM-1083 | Cayley boundary | Subdivision transit vertices generally are not group elements or original Schreier states. | Original action semantics retained only at branch vertices. | Note 125 |
| SEM-1084 | Complexity boundary | Literal subdivision adds `sum(ell(e)-1)` vertices and replaces original edges by `sum ell(e)` unit edges. | One internally disjoint path per edge. | Direct count; note 125 |
| SEM-1085 | Algorithm boundary | Integer-weight shortest paths can be processed without materializing a subdivision. | Suitable nonnegative integer-weight algorithm and declared bounds. | Dial 1969; note 125 |
| SEM-1086 | Distributed boundary | Degree-two transit chains can add rounds and owner crossings without adding logical branch choices. | Expanded graph is physically traversed. | Note 125 |
| SEM-1087 | Reporting rule | Weighted logical graph, expanded unit graph, branch-only outputs, transit work, replay, and partition costs are separate claims. | Representation and query domain declared. | Note 125 |
| SEM-1088 | Proved pivot bound | One complete BFS gives `ecc(x)<=d(s,x)+ecc(s)` and `ecc(x)>=max(d(s,x),ecc(s)-d(s,x))`. | Finite connected undirected graph. | Triangle inequalities; note 126 |
| SEM-1089 | Proved sweep monotonicity | If `v` is farthest from `u`, then `ecc(v)>=ecc(u)`. | Undirected distance symmetry. | `ecc(v)>=d(v,u)=ecc(u)`; note 126 |
| SEM-1090 | Rejected implication | Equal eccentricities in consecutive farthest sweeps certify diameter. | A sweep chain can plateau below the global maximum. | Note 21 counterexample and note 126 |
| SEM-1091 | Lower-bound contract | Every complete double-sweep result is a valid diameter lower bound. | Connected undirected graph and complete BFS layers. | Returned distance is one graph distance; note 126 |
| SEM-1092 | Tie boundary | Farthest-vertex tie policy can change later sweep roots and bounds without invalidating the current lower bound. | Complete farthest set or declared selection policy. | Note 126 |
| SEM-1093 | Proved multi-pivot lower bound | Maximum known pivot eccentricity is a nondecreasing diameter lower bound. | Complete pivot BFS sweeps. | Definition of diameter; note 126 |
| SEM-1094 | Proved multi-pivot upper bound | `D<=max_x min_p(d(p,x)+ecc(p))`. | Complete distance fields for every declared pivot and full vertex domain. | Triangle inequality; note 126 |
| SEM-1095 | Exactness certificate | Matching global lower and upper diameter bounds certify exact diameter. | Both inequalities valid for one graph epoch. | Note 126 |
| SEM-1096 | Proved fringe bound | After all vertices at root depth at least `i` are swept, `D<=max(M,2(i-1))`. | Complete root layering and complete eccentricities for the processed outer layers. | Outer endpoint or inner-pair case split; note 126 |
| SEM-1097 | Rejected implication | Sampling some outer-fringe vertices preserves the fringe upper bound. | Unswept outer endpoints may have larger eccentricity. | Note 126 |
| SEM-1098 | Heuristic boundary | Four-sweep midpoint selection affects initial bounds and work but is not itself a center or exact-diameter certificate. | General undirected graph. | Crescenzi et al.; note 126 |
| SEM-1099 | Worst-case boundary | iFUB can require `Theta(nm)` work despite using few BFS sweeps on many measured real-world graphs. | General undirected unweighted input. | Crescenzi et al.; note 126 |
| SEM-1100 | Tree upper bound | The diameter of any BFS spanning tree upper-bounds graph diameter. | Connected undirected graph and spanning BFS tree. | Subgraph distance monotonicity; note 126 |
| SEM-1101 | Rejected implication | Some BFS tree must have tree diameter equal to graph diameter. | Parent-tree detours can remain unavoidable. | Crescenzi et al.; note 126 |
| SEM-1102 | Directed boundary | Undirected farthest-sweep monotonicity and fringe proofs do not transfer automatically to directed out-distance. | Symmetry and finite-distance assumptions fail. | Note 126 |
| SEM-1103 | Cayley fact | One complete identity BFS gives diameter in a finite connected undirected Cayley graph. | Exact group states and inverse-closed connected generator graph. | Translation symmetry; notes 21 and 126 |
| SEM-1104 | Reporting rule | Per-root traversal, concurrent roots, sequential root selection, bound reduction, and exact certificate time are separate GPU/multi-GPU claims. | Complete per-root evidence and one graph epoch. | Note 126 |
| SEM-1105 | Definition | A simple complement flips adjacency on every pair of distinct vertices and excludes loops. | Finite simple undirected graph. | Note 127 |
| SEM-1106 | Exact pair fact | An original nonedge has complement distance one; an original edge has complement distance two exactly when its endpoints have a common original nonneighbor. | Distinct vertices in a simple graph. | Note 127 |
| SEM-1107 | Rejected formula | Complement distance is a fixed arithmetic inverse or monotone transform of original distance. | Complement can disconnect or collapse large diameter. | `P5`, `K_n`; note 127 |
| SEM-1108 | Proved connectivity fact | If `G` is disconnected on at least two vertices, its complement is connected with diameter at most two. | Simple undirected graph. | Cross-component adjacency proof; note 127 |
| SEM-1109 | Proved exact diameter fact | For disconnected `G`, complement diameter is one iff `G` is edgeless, otherwise two. | At least two vertices. | Note 127 |
| SEM-1110 | Co-component fact | Distinct complement components are completely joined to one another in the original graph. | Simple complement convention. | Definition of complement; note 127 |
| SEM-1111 | Proved diameter fact | `diam(G)>=4` implies `diam(bar(G))=2`. | Finite connected simple undirected graph. | Common-nonneighbor contrapositive; note 127 |
| SEM-1112 | Frontier identity | Complement next frontier is `U_i \ intersection_(v in F_i) N_G(v)`. | Completed current layer and authoritative unvisited set. | Quantifier conversion; note 127 |
| SEM-1113 | Rejected frontier rule | Subtracting the union of original frontier neighborhoods computes the complement next frontier. | One candidate may be adjacent to one frontier vertex and nonadjacent to another. | Two-frontier counterexample; note 127 |
| SEM-1114 | Visited boundary | Original and complement BFS visited sets are different graph-epoch evidence even on identical vertex IDs. | Metric and adjacency version included in identity. | Note 127 |
| SEM-1115 | Representation fact | A sparse graph may have a quadratic-size complement, yet complement BFS need not materialize all complement edges. | Suitable implicit representation. | Ito-Yokoyama; note 127 |
| SEM-1116 | Accounting boundary | Original adjacency inspections, nonedge tests, unvisited operations, logical complement edges, and materialized edges are different work counts. | Representation declared. | Note 127 |
| SEM-1117 | Cayley complement fact | The simple directed complement of `Cay(Gamma,S)` is `Cay(Gamma,Gamma\({e} union S))`. | Finite group, loop-free right Cayley convention. | Generator-set complement; note 127 |
| SEM-1118 | Rejected transfer | A short complement-Cayley path is a short path in the original generator metric. | Complement generators are original nonmoves. | Note 127 |
| SEM-1119 | Schreier boundary | Complementing an underlying simple action graph does not automatically yield a meaningful generator-labeled Schreier graph. | Labels and action realization required. | Note 127 |
| SEM-1120 | Distributed correctness boundary | Missing local adjacency is not proof of a complement edge when original edges are sharded. | Authoritative global membership or nonedge evidence required. | Note 127 |
| SEM-1121 | Reporting rule | Complement construction, logical adjacency, candidate work, visited ownership, communication, traversal, and validation are separate claims. | Graph and representation epoch declared. | Note 127 |
| SEM-1122 | Definition | Strong labeled bisimulation preserves observations and matches every labeled outgoing transition in both directions. | Declared transition system and observation map. | Baier-Katoen; note 128 |
| SEM-1123 | Path-lifting fact | Every quotient path under a bisimulation equivalence lifts stepwise from any representative of its initial class. | Strong same-label bisimulation and declared quotient self-loop convention. | Induction on path length; note 128 |
| SEM-1124 | Exact distance fact | A bisimulation quotient preserves minimum unit-step distance from a concrete start to a class-saturated goal set. | Goal membership is an observation and quotient path lifting holds. | Projection plus lifting; note 128 |
| SEM-1125 | Rejected fixed-target claim | Bisimulation quotient distance to `[t]` automatically equals distance to one concrete representative `t`. | A lift may reach another class member. | Note 17 reflected path; note 128 |
| SEM-1126 | Simulation boundary | One-way simulation can project concrete behavior into an over-approximation but does not guarantee lifting of every abstract path. | Direction of simulation and target map declared. | Baier-Katoen; note 128 |
| SEM-1127 | Rejected exactness | Simulation alone licenses exact BFS on merged states. | Abstract reachability may be spurious. | Note 128 |
| SEM-1128 | Multiplicity boundary | Plain bisimulation preserves existence of class transitions, not degree, parallel multiplicity, or shortest-path count. | Existential matching clauses. | Note 128 |
| SEM-1129 | Partition relation | Every equitable partition of a finite simple graph is an unlabeled graph bisimulation. | Equal neighbor counts into every cell. | Positive-count matching; note 128 |
| SEM-1130 | Rejected converse | Every graph bisimulation partition is equitable. | Bisimulation ignores counts. | Universal class on `P3`; note 128 |
| SEM-1131 | Observation boundary | Without state observations, universal bisimulation can collapse every state of an unlabeled transition system with no dead ends. | One common transition label and every state has an outgoing transition. | Existential step matching; note 128 |
| SEM-1132 | Cost boundary | Unit-step bisimulation does not preserve weighted shortest cost unless matched transitions preserve the relevant cost. | Weighted transition system. | Note 128 |
| SEM-1133 | Stutter boundary | Collapsing internal stutter steps can preserve selected temporal properties while changing BFS hop distance. | Stutter rather than strong step bisimulation. | Baier-Katoen; note 128 |
| SEM-1134 | Directed boundary | Outgoing bisimulation supports forward traversal but not automatically reverse or bidirectional BFS. | Incoming transitions may have different class behavior. | Note 128 |
| SEM-1135 | Automorphism fact | Label-preserving automorphism orbits form bisimulation classes and equitable cells in finite simple graphs. | Automorphism group preserves declared labels. | Neighbor bijection; note 128 |
| SEM-1136 | Rejected symmetry claim | Every bisimulation class is an automorphism orbit. | Behavioral equivalence is weaker than global symmetry. | Note 128 |
| SEM-1137 | Cayley congruence | A deterministic labeled Cayley quotient is well-defined when equivalence is preserved by every right generator action. | Declared action side and generator alphabet. | Note 128 |
| SEM-1138 | Visited boundary | Class-level visited proves quotient reachability and saturated-goal distance, not enumeration or fixed-representative distances inside each class. | Valid quotient and output contract. | Note 128 |
| SEM-1139 | Reporting rule | Partition proof, observations, saturation, concrete/class candidates, lifting, ownership, and original/quotient costs are separate GPU/multi-GPU claims. | One transition and partition epoch. | Note 128 |
| SEM-1140 | Definition | Two DFA states are residual-language equivalent exactly when every suffix is accepted or rejected identically from them. | Deterministic automaton and fixed acceptance set. | Myhill-Nerode; note 129 |
| SEM-1141 | Congruence fact | Residual-language equivalence respects acceptance and every symbol successor. | Complete DFA, with partial transitions interpreted through a rejecting sink. | Language derivatives; note 129 |
| SEM-1142 | Classical theorem | A language is regular iff its Myhill-Nerode relation has finitely many classes; reachable classes form the unique minimal DFA up to renaming. | Ordinary finite-word language. | Myhill-Nerode theorem; note 129 |
| SEM-1143 | BFS fact | Visited-once by DFA state preserves existence and minimum length of an accepted word. | Unit symbol cost, deterministic transitions, acceptance checked at depth zero. | Residual future independence; note 129 |
| SEM-1144 | Enumeration boundary | DFA-state visited does not enumerate all accepted words or prefixes. | Multiple words may reach one state and cycles may generate infinitely many words. | Note 129 |
| SEM-1145 | Language preservation | Minimal-DFA quotient preserves the accepted word set, minimum accepted length, per-length word counts, and fixed-order shortlex minimum. | Equivalent complete DFA and fixed alphabet order. | Note 129 |
| SEM-1146 | Rejected merge rule | Equal distance to acceptance implies equal residual language. | States can accept different one-letter suffixes at the same minimum distance. | `a` versus `b` suffix counterexample; note 129 |
| SEM-1147 | Bisimulation relation | In a complete deterministic labeled automaton with acceptance observation, residual equivalence is the coarsest strong same-label bisimulation. | Total deterministic transitions. | Successor derivatives and trace induction; note 129 |
| SEM-1148 | Partial-DFA boundary | Missing transitions must retain implicit rejecting-sink semantics during minimization. | Partial DFA contract. | Note 129 |
| SEM-1149 | NFA boundary | The minimal-DFA theorem applies after deterministic subset-state semantics, not as naive merging of individual NFA states. | Nondeterministic automaton. | Note 129 |
| SEM-1150 | Epsilon boundary | Charging epsilon transitions as unit BFS edges changes accepted word length. | Epsilon consumes no alphabet symbol. | Note 129 |
| SEM-1151 | Product preservation | Replacing a constraint DFA by its minimal equivalent preserves accepted base walks and their minimum length in labeled graph product BFS. | Same base graph, target, alphabet, and language. | Note 129 |
| SEM-1152 | Product identity boundary | Automaton minimization merges residual memory classes but does not merge distinct base vertices. | Product key `(v,[q])`. | Note 129 |
| SEM-1153 | Reachability boundary | Standalone minimal-DFA reachability and reachable states in one particular graph product need not coincide. | Base graph may realize only part of the alphabet language. | Note 129 |
| SEM-1154 | Cayley boundary | Minimizing a regular constraint automaton preserves its language but does not prove coverage, uniqueness, geodesicity, or prefix closure under group evaluation. | Declared Cayley/Schreier action. | Notes 99 and 129 |
| SEM-1155 | Reverse boundary | A minimized forward DFA is not automatically deterministic under reversed transitions. | Symbols may have multiple predecessor states. | Note 129 |
| SEM-1156 | Counting boundary | DFA states, residual classes, prefixes, accepted words, base walks, product states, and base states are distinct counts. | Output contract declared. | Note 129 |
| SEM-1157 | Reporting rule | DFA trim/minimization, product traversal, class ownership, accepted words, concrete paths, replay, and preprocessing costs are separate GPU/multi-GPU claims. | One automaton and graph epoch. | Note 129 |
| SEM-1158 | Subset-construction fact | After word `w`, the deterministic NFA configuration is exactly the set of states reachable by some run labeled `w`. | Epsilon-free NFA or declared epsilon closure. | Rabin-Scott; note 130 |
| SEM-1159 | BFS fact | BFS over reachable NFA subsets finds minimum accepted word length. | Unit symbol cost and existential acceptance. | Subset determinization; note 130 |
| SEM-1160 | Identity boundary | Sharing one active NFA member does not make two subset configurations equal. | Full active set determines future language. | Note 130 |
| SEM-1161 | Rejected frontier merge | Unioning all subset configurations in one word-depth frontier preserves their word semantics. | The subsets belong to different prefixes. | `{p}`/`{q}` suffix counterexample; note 130 |
| SEM-1162 | Capacity boundary | An `n`-state NFA can have exponentially many reachable deterministic subset states. | General finite NFA. | Rabin-Scott powerset construction; note 130 |
| SEM-1163 | Monotonicity fact | `P subset R` implies `post_z(P) subset post_z(R)` and existential suffix language `L(P) subset L(R)`. | Same NFA and symbol word `z`. | Union monotonicity; note 130 |
| SEM-1164 | Qualified dominance | At the same base state, a superset `R` reached no later than `P subset R` dominates `P` for existence/length of one shortest existentially accepted continuation. | Same remaining semantics and target; no enumeration or shortlex claim. | Note 130 |
| SEM-1165 | Rejected dominance | Any superset subset-state dominates a subset regardless of depth or base vertex. | Later prefixes or different base suffix graphs can lose optimality. | Note 130 |
| SEM-1166 | Output boundary | Inclusion dominance for one shortest accepted path automatically preserves all words, shortlex order, or NFA-run provenance. | These outputs depend on discarded prefixes or runs. | Note 130 |
| SEM-1167 | Rejected fingerprint | Equal subset size or large overlap proves subset equality, language equivalence, or dominance. | Membership identity and residual behavior may differ. | Note 130 |
| SEM-1168 | Reduction taxonomy | Exact subset equality, inclusion dominance, and residual-language equivalence are distinct reductions. | Their premises and outputs are declared separately. | Notes 129-130 |
| SEM-1169 | Epsilon boundary | Epsilon closure consumes zero word length and must finalize before the next symbol-depth layer. | Epsilon-NFA word metric. | Note 130 |
| SEM-1170 | Multiplicity boundary | Subset construction preserves accepted words but not the number of accepting NFA runs. | Existential set semantics. | Note 130 |
| SEM-1171 | Product identity | Exact constrained graph state is `(base vertex, active NFA subset)`. | Labeled graph and NFA product. | Note 130 |
| SEM-1172 | Cayley boundary | NFA determinization preserves the regular constraint language but not uniqueness under group or Schreier evaluation. | Declared action and state equality. | Notes 99 and 130 |
| SEM-1173 | Bidirectional boundary | Forward subset and reverse subset require a compatibility relation at a base-state meeting. | Reversed NFA semantics and accepted whole word. | Note 130 |
| SEM-1174 | Distributed correctness boundary | A partial per-owner union of member successors is not an authoritative next subset. | Sharded NFA transitions or product generation. | Note 130 |
| SEM-1175 | Reporting rule | NFA, subsets, antichain/dominance evidence, words, runs, product/base states, ownership, and preprocessing costs are separate GPU/multi-GPU claims. | One automaton and graph epoch. | Note 130 |
| SEM-1176 | Definition | A reachability-game attractor is the least fixed point adding existential vertices with some winning successor and universal vertices with all successors winning. | Finite turn-based arena and declared dead-end convention. | Mazala; Fijalkow; note 131 |
| SEM-1177 | Rank fact | Attractor layer `A_k` is exactly the set from which the reachability player can force a winning terminal within at most `k` moves. | Finite total arena with target terminals, or dead ends explicitly included in the winning convention. | Layer induction; note 131 |
| SEM-1178 | Minimax recurrence | Winning rank is `1+min` successor rank at existential vertices and `1+max` at universal vertices when all successors are winning. | Unit moves and finite ranks. | Note 131 |
| SEM-1179 | Special case | If every vertex is existential, attractor rank equals ordinary directed distance to the target set. | Reverse multi-source BFS semantics. | Note 131 |
| SEM-1180 | Over-approximation | Ordinary reverse reachability contains the reachability-game attractor. | Same directed graph and target; universal ownership ignored by reachability. | Note 131 |
| SEM-1181 | Rejected implication | A universal vertex with one shortest edge to the target is winning. | Another successor may enter a target-avoiding trap. | `u->t`, `u->c->c`; note 131 |
| SEM-1182 | Fixed-point boundary | A universal self-loop plus a target edge does not justify winning under the least attractor fixed point. | Infinite target-avoiding plays lose for the reachability player. | Note 131 |
| SEM-1183 | Classical fact | Finite reachability games are positionally determined. | Turn-based perfect-information reachability objective. | Mazala; Fijalkow; note 131 |
| SEM-1184 | Trap fact | Outside the attractor, existential vertices have no edge into it and universal vertices have an adversarial successor outside it. | Stabilized least fixed point. | Note 131 |
| SEM-1185 | Dead-end boundary | Vacuous universal predecessor truth matches player-to-move-loses semantics but not every deadlock convention. | Owner and terminal semantics declared. | Note 131 |
| SEM-1186 | Finalization boundary | Existential winning status needs one winning successor; universal winning status needs all authoritative successors winning. | Complete graph epoch. | Note 131 |
| SEM-1187 | Error polarity | Missing/spurious edges can cause opposite false-positive/false-negative effects at existential and universal vertices. | Ownership and successor truth declared. | Four-case analysis; note 131 |
| SEM-1188 | Witness boundary | One favorable path through universal vertices is not a winning-strategy certificate. | Strategy must cover every adversary response. | Note 131 |
| SEM-1189 | Counting boundary | Path counts and strategy counts are different because one strategy induces a branching family of plays. | Game and counting convention declared. | Note 131 |
| SEM-1190 | Alternation boundary | Replacing an AND/universal node by ordinary adjacency changes all-successors acceptance into some-successor reachability. | Alternating or AND/OR semantics. | Note 131 |
| SEM-1191 | Cayley boundary | Adversarial ownership or control phase changes Cayley word distance into forced-reachability game rank. | Expanded game state and compatible symmetry declared. | Note 131 |
| SEM-1192 | Distributed correctness boundary | Local completion of a sharded universal successor set is not global all-successor evidence. | Authoritative outdegree and consistent graph epoch. | Note 131 |
| SEM-1193 | Reporting rule | Arena, reachability, attractor, ranks, strategy, universal certificates, fixed-point rounds, and GPU/multi-GPU costs are separate claims. | Ownership and terminal contract declared. | Note 131 |
| SEM-1194 | Exact support fact | Finite support reachability is equivalent to positive-probability finite-time target reachability. | Fixed Markov chain; support edge iff probability is positive. | Note 132 |
| SEM-1195 | Distance interpretation | Support BFS distance is the earliest horizon with nonzero first-hit probability. | Discrete-time fixed chain and target set. | Note 132 |
| SEM-1196 | Rejected implication | Finite support distance implies almost-sure target reachability. | A positive-probability branch may enter an absorbing target-free trap. | Note 132 |
| SEM-1197 | Counterexample | Identical support distance one can have eventual hit probability `1/2` or `1`. | Absorbing-trap versus geometric self-loop chains. | Note 132 |
| SEM-1198 | Finite-chain criterion | Almost-sure target reachability is equivalent to every reachable BSCC intersecting the absorbing target. | Finite Markov chain; target totalized as absorbing. | Baier–Katoen; note 132 |
| SEM-1199 | Numerical boundary | Hitting probabilities satisfy weighted harmonic equations and are not recoverable from BFS levels alone. | Transition probabilities retained. | Note 132 |
| SEM-1200 | Expectation boundary | Hit probability below one makes unconditional expected hitting time infinite under the standard infinite-on-non-hit convention. | Extended nonnegative hitting time. | Note 132 |
| SEM-1201 | Finite-state boundary | Almost-sure target hitting implies finite expected hitting time in a finite chain, but not in every infinite-state chain. | Fixed finite transition matrix. | Note 132 |
| SEM-1202 | Bounded-horizon boundary | Support BFS stores only the first positive entry of a weighted bounded-reachability sequence. | Exact positive support. | Note 132 |
| SEM-1203 | MDP boundary | MDP reachability quantifies over policies and then averages over Nature; it is neither ordinary existential BFS nor universal adversarial reachability. | Enabled actions and transition distributions declared. | Parker; note 132 |
| SEM-1204 | Strategy fact | Memoryless deterministic policies suffice for optimal finite-MDP reachability values. | Finite reachability MDP. | Kwiatkowska–Norman–Parker; note 132 |
| SEM-1205 | Quantifier separation | A probabilistic self-loop can be escaped almost surely although an adversary could select it forever. | Fixed exit probability is positive. | Note 132 |
| SEM-1206 | Rejected optimization equivalence | A shortest support path need not maximize eventual success probability or minimize expected hitting time. | Weighted stochastic outcomes. | Note 132 |
| SEM-1207 | Cayley boundary | Random-generator support BFS yields a word metric, not hitting probability, expected time, mixing, or cover time. | Positive-probability generator labels declared. | Notes 95 and 132 |
| SEM-1208 | Multiplicity boundary | Parallel labels sharing an endpoint may be deduplicated for support reachability but their probability masses must be combined for stochastic analysis. | Labeled transition distribution. | Note 132 |
| SEM-1209 | Tolerance boundary | Omitting a tiny positive edge into a trap can reverse an almost-sure classification. | Exact model support versus declared approximation distinguished. | Note 132 |
| SEM-1210 | Distributed correctness boundary | Local row normalization does not prove a sharded global transition row is complete or sums to one. | Complete graph epoch and global mass reduction. | Note 132 |
| SEM-1211 | Reporting rule | Support BFS, recurrent-component analysis, qualitative classification, numerical values, expectation, policy, convergence, communication, and timing are separate claims. | Model and numerical certificate declared. | Note 132 |

| SEM-1330 | Validated observation | `CT_8` layers are `[1,28,322,1960,6769,13132,13068,5040]`. | Identity-root exact BFS. | Docker probe; note 138 |
| SEM-1331 | Controlled comparison | On `S_8`, adjacent/pancake/star/all-transposition degrees are `7/7/7/28` and diameters `28/9/10/7`. | Same permutation universe; declared generators. | Notes 10, 136–138 |
| SEM-1332 | Tradeoff boundary | Adding generators can reduce diameter while increasing candidate degree and duplicate work. | Logical and hardware costs kept separate. | Note 138 |
| SEM-1333 | Normal-Cayley fact | All transpositions are conjugacy-invariant, so identity distance is a conjugacy-class function. | Full transposition class as generators. | Note 138 |
| SEM-1334 | Identity boundary | Conjugacy/cycle type determines scalar distance but is not complete permutation identity. | Target and output contract declared. | Note 138 |
| SEM-1335 | Witness boundary | Exact cycle-count distance, one minimal factorization, shortest-DAG parents, and factorization counts are distinct outputs. | Composition and labels declared. | Note 138 |
| SEM-1336 | GPU capacity boundary | Closed distance and diameter formulas do not remove factorial visited capacity for exhaustive BFS. | Full state traversal. | Note 138 |
| SEM-1337 | Reporting rule | Degree, swap attempts, oracle/rank work, histories, duplicates, visited, inward/outward edges, routing, synchronization, and end-to-end time are separate claims. | Logical and execution contract declared. | Note 138 |
| SEM-1338 | Proven | Multi-source BFS from a fixed center set computes exact distance to that set and its finite covering radius after complete-universe reachability is established. | Standard BFS induction applied to all centers at depth zero. | Note 139 |
| SEM-1339 | Proven | Ordinary domination is closed radius-one coverage; distance domination is graph-ball coverage at the declared radius. | Definitions rewritten as `rho(C)<=r`. | Note 139 |
| SEM-1340 | Boundary | Evaluating a fixed center set, minimizing center count, and minimizing radius with at most `k` centers are distinct problems. | Output and optimization contracts separated. | Note 139 |
| SEM-1341 | Rejected universal claim | A maximum-degree vertex need not be an optimal one-center. | Exhaustive eight-vertex Docker/Rust fixture: radius 4 versus optimum 3. | Note 139; REF-033 |
| SEM-1342 | Rejected exactness claim | Farthest-first need not solve graph k-center exactly. | Endpoint-seeded `P6`: greedy radius 2 versus exhaustive optimum 1. | Note 139; REF-033 |
| SEM-1343 | Theorem | Farthest-first has covering radius at most twice optimum in a metric. | Classical packing/pigeonhole proof under triangle inequality. | Note 139; Gonzalez 1985 |
| SEM-1344 | Certificate boundary | A proposed center set gives an upper bound; a sufficiently separated `k+1` point packing gives a lower bound. | Ball coverage and triangle inequality. | Note 139 |
| SEM-1345 | Correctness rule | A component without a center makes undirected covering radius infinite; reducing only reached labels is unsound. | Disconnected fixture returns no finite radius. | Note 139; REF-033 |
| SEM-1346 | Boundary | Voronoi tie-breaking can change ownership but not scalar distance to the center set or covering radius. | Minimum-distance semantics separated from owner metadata. | Notes 13 and 139 |
| SEM-1347 | Direction rule | Directed outward coverage and distance-to-facility coverage use opposite graph orientations. | Forward/reverse BFS distance definitions. | Notes 13 and 139 |
| SEM-1348 | Cayley boundary | Vertex transitivity trivializes singleton placement but not relative placement of multiple centers. | Common translation preserves pairwise arrangement. | Note 139 |
| SEM-1349 | Measurement rule | Iterative center selection must report traversal count and total work separately from one BFS evaluation. | Evaluator and selector costs separated. | Note 139 |
| SEM-1350 | Boundary | Ordinary BFS minimizes hop count but its first parent need not minimize a secondary additive cost among minimum-hop paths. | Six-vertex exhaustive witness: parent cost 200 versus optimum 2 at depth 2. | Note 140; REF-034 |
| SEM-1351 | Proven | Edges with finite `d(u)` satisfying `d(v)=d(u)+1` form a depth-acyclic shortest-path subgraph containing every minimum-hop path from the root. | Exact BFS labels; strict finite depth increase, not infinity-sentinel equality. | Notes 11, 41, and 140 |
| SEM-1352 | Proven | Minimum additive secondary cost among minimum-hop paths is computable by depth-ordered dynamic programming over the shortest-path DAG. | All shortest predecessors retained; finite arithmetic. | Note 140 |
| SEM-1353 | Finalization rule | A secondary improvement at fixed BFS depth must be incorporated before dependent expansion or repropagated to descendants. | Distance finality does not imply richer-label finality. | Notes 13 and 140 |
| SEM-1354 | Rejected equivalence | Hop-first and cost-first path objectives need not select the same path. | Exhaustive fixture: `(2,2)` versus `(3,0)`. | Note 140; REF-034 |
| SEM-1355 | Rejected single-label claim | One label per vertex cannot in general represent all Pareto hop/cost trade-offs. | Incomparable fixture labels `(2,2)` and `(3,0)`. | Note 140; REF-034 |
| SEM-1356 | Scope fact | Negative secondary edge costs cause no cycle ambiguity inside the shortest-hop DAG because every retained edge strictly increases depth. | Finite exact BFS layering; arithmetic still bounded. | Note 140 |
| SEM-1357 | State boundary | Resource/history fields affecting future feasibility must be retained in product-state identity or an equivalent nondominated-label contract. | Vertex-only merging can discard feasible continuations. | Notes 20 and 140 |
| SEM-1358 | Output boundary | One secondary-optimal parent, deterministic parent, all optimal parents, and optimal-path counts are different outputs. | Metadata and reduction contracts separated. | Note 140 |
| SEM-1359 | Cayley boundary | Translation of hop distances does not transfer a state-dependent secondary-cost rule unless that rule is also translation invariant. | Fixed action/generator and cost convention. | Note 140 |
| SEM-1360 | Distributed rule | Message-order first arrival cannot provide minimum-secondary shortest parents; owners need complete equal-depth reduction or repropagation. | Sharded exact traversal with richer output contract. | Note 140 |
| SEM-1361 | Reporting rule | Traversal, shortest-DAG filtering, secondary relaxations, label multiplicity, routing, and end-to-end time are separate claims. | Semantic and execution work separated. | Note 140 |
| SEM-1362 | Proven | `H(d,q)` has `q^d` vertices, degree `d(q-1)`, Hamming graph distance, and diameter `d`. | Every move replaces one coordinate; direct coordinate correction attains the lower bound. | Note 141 |
| SEM-1363 | Proven | The depth-`i` BFS frontier has size `C(d,i)(q-1)^i`. | Choose differing coordinates and their nonzero symbols. | Note 141 |
| SEM-1364 | Proven | Consecutive Hamming frontier ratio is `((d-i)(q-1))/(i+1)` and normalized depths are binomial with parameter `(q-1)/q`. | Algebraic consequence of SEM-1363. | Note 141 |
| SEM-1365 | Proven | A depth-`i` state has intersection counts `c_i=i`, `a_i=i(q-2)`, and `b_i=(d-i)(q-1)`. | Coordinate-change classification. | Note 141 |
| SEM-1366 | Proven boundary | `H(d,2)` is bipartite, whereas every `H(d,q)` with `d>=1,q>2` contains coordinate triangles and same-layer edges. | `a_i=0` only for `q=2`; each coordinate induces `K_q`. | Note 141 |
| SEM-1367 | Proven | Complete expansion of `F_i` produces exactly `i+1` outward candidate occurrences per semantic state in `F_(i+1)`. | Intersection edge balance. | Note 141 |
| SEM-1368 | Proven | Every depth-`i` Hamming word has exactly `i!` shortest paths from zero. | Each differing coordinate is set directly once in arbitrary order. | Note 141 |
| SEM-1369 | Validated observation | All distance, layer, intersection, and path-count formulas matched complete BFS for `d=1..5,q=2..4`. | Fifteen Docker/Rust fixtures, at most 1,024 states. | Note 141; REF-035 |
| SEM-1370 | Spectral fact | `H(d,q)` has eigenvalues `d(q-1)-qj` with multiplicities `C(d,j)(q-1)^j`. | Cartesian product of `d` copies of `K_q`. | Note 141; Brouwer-Cohen-Neumaier |
| SEM-1371 | Representation boundary | Dense base-`q` ranking enables exact bitmap identity but does not remove exponential `q^d` capacity. | Full word state and bijective positional encoding. | Note 141 |
| SEM-1372 | Calibration boundary | Hamming regularity predicts logical work but not GPU locality, ranking cost, owner traffic, or irregular puzzle behavior. | Workload transfer must preserve representation and execution assumptions. | Note 141 |
| SEM-1373 | Reporting rule | Inward, same-layer, outward, duplicate, remote, synchronization, and end-to-end quantities are separate claims. | Semantic intersection counts versus physical execution. | Note 141 |
| SEM-1374 | Proven | `J(n,k)` has `C(n,k)` vertices, degree `k(n-k)`, distance half symmetric difference, and diameter `min(k,n-k)`. | One selected/unselected exchange repairs at most one mismatch and the bound is attainable. | Note 142 |
| SEM-1375 | Proven | The depth-`i` Johnson frontier has size `C(k,i)C(n-k,i)` and the layers sum to `C(n,k)`. | Choose omitted root elements and inserted outside elements; Vandermonde identity. | Note 142 |
| SEM-1376 | Proven | Consecutive frontier ratio is `((k-i)(n-k-i))/(i+1)^2`. | Algebraic consequence of SEM-1375. | Note 142 |
| SEM-1377 | Proven | A depth-`i` state has `c_i=i^2`, `a_i=i(n-2i)`, and `b_i=(k-i)(n-k-i)`. | Membership-category classification of one exchange. | Note 142 |
| SEM-1378 | Proven boundary | Nontrivial Johnson graphs with `n>=3` contain same-layer edges and triangles; fixed cardinality does not imply bipartiteness. | `a_1=n-2` and common `(k-1)`-subset construction. | Note 142 |
| SEM-1379 | Proven | Each state in `F_(i+1)` receives exactly `(i+1)^2` outward candidate occurrences from `F_i`. | Johnson intersection edge balance. | Note 142 |
| SEM-1380 | Proven | Every depth-`i` Johnson target has exactly `(i!)^2` shortest paths. | Independent removal and insertion orders. | Note 142 |
| SEM-1381 | Representation boundary | A Johnson edge is a two-bit exchange; the fixed-weight induced subgraph of the ordinary hypercube has no edges. | One-bit flips leave the fixed-weight state universe. | Note 142 |
| SEM-1382 | Schreier boundary | All transpositions acting on `k`-subsets include `k(n-k)` moving occurrences and `C(k,2)+C(n-k,2)` stabilizer occurrences. | Labeled transposition action versus simple Johnson adjacency. | Note 142 |
| SEM-1383 | Validated observation | All Johnson formulas matched complete BFS for every normalized `J(n,k)` with `2<=n<=12`. | Thirty-six Docker/Rust fixtures, at most 924 states. | Note 142; REF-036 |
| SEM-1384 | Identity boundary | Root intersection size determines scalar distance but is not a complete visited key for `k`-subsets. | Many distinct subsets share one intersection size. | Note 142 |
| SEM-1385 | Spectral fact | Johnson adjacency eigenvalues are `k(n-k)-j(n+1-j)` with multiplicity `C(n,j)-C(n,j-1)`. | `0<=j<=min(k,n-k)`. | Note 142; Brouwer-Cohen-Neumaier |
| SEM-1386 | Calibration boundary | Johnson regularity and closed occurrence counts do not determine combinadic cost, owner locality, or puzzle performance. | Representation and execution assumptions remain workload-specific. | Note 142 |
| SEM-1387 | Reporting rule | Stabilizer loops, inward/same/outward occurrences, new states, remote traffic, synchronization, and end-to-end time are separate claims. | Schreier semantics versus physical execution. | Note 142 |
| SEM-1388 | Proven | `J_q(n,k)` has `[n choose k]_q` subspace vertices, degree `q[k]_q[n-k]_q`, distance `k-dim(U intersection W)`, and diameter `min(k,n-k)`. | Finite-field subspace counting and one-dimension exchange. | Note 143 |
| SEM-1389 | Proven | The depth-`i` Grassmann frontier has `q^(i^2)[k choose i]_q[n-k choose i]_q` states. | Intersection-deficit decomposition of the finite Grassmannian. | Note 143 |
| SEM-1390 | Proven | Consecutive Grassmann frontier ratio is `q^(2i+1)[k-i]_q[n-k-i]_q/[i+1]_q^2`. | Gaussian-binomial ratio. | Note 143 |
| SEM-1391 | Proven | Grassmann intersection numbers satisfy `c_i=[i]_q^2` and `b_i=q^(2i+1)[k-i]_q[n-k-i]_q`. | Distance-regular Grassmann scheme. | Note 143; Terwilliger 2020 |
| SEM-1392 | Proven | Every depth-`i` Grassmann target has `([i]_q!)^2` shortest graph paths. | Product of inward predecessor counts. | Note 143 |
| SEM-1393 | Metric boundary | Constant-dimension subspace distance is twice Grassmann graph distance. | Both subspaces have dimension `k`. | Note 143 |
| SEM-1394 | q-analog boundary | Formal `q->1` limits of Grassmann layers, intersection counts, and path counts recover Johnson formulas. | Algebraic limit, not a finite-field execution. | Notes 142 and 143 |
| SEM-1395 | Identity rule | Different full-rank bases with the same row space are one Grassmann vertex and must deduplicate in exact visited semantics. | Fixed field and canonical subspace equality. | Note 143 |
| SEM-1396 | Representation fact | Each `k`-subspace has `|GL(k,q)|` ordered bases. | Full-rank ordered basis representation. | Note 143 |
| SEM-1397 | Schreier boundary | `GL(n,q)` acts transitively on `k`-subspaces with a large stabilizer; group-operation occurrences are not distinct graph neighbors. | Natural linear action. | Note 143 |
| SEM-1398 | Validated observation | All binary Grassmann formulas matched complete BFS for normalized `n<=6`. | Nine Docker/Rust fixtures, at most 1,395 states. | Note 143; REF-037 |
| SEM-1399 | Calibration boundary | Closed Grassmann graph work does not determine basis-generation, canonicalization, rank, owner locality, or hardware throughput. | Representation and execution remain separate. | Note 143 |
| SEM-1400 | Distributed rule | Equal subspaces must route by stable canonical identity; raw basis ownership can split semantic duplicates across owners. | Exact sharded visited contract. | Note 143 |
| SEM-1401 | Reporting rule | Field operations, basis candidates, canonicalization, distinct neighbors, visited, routing, synchronization, and end-to-end time are separate claims. | Semantic and physical work decomposition. | Note 143 |
| SEM-1402 | Model boundary | Exact BFS runs on one frozen random-graph sample; resampling adjacency on query defines a different temporal/annealed process. | Stable symmetric edge decisions required. | Note 144 |
| SEM-1403 | Qualified fact | Local sparse `G(n,c/n)` exploration is approximated by a Poisson-`c` branching process before depletion and collisions become material. | Asymptotic local scope, not a deterministic frontier equation. | Note 144 |
| SEM-1404 | Classical fact | The Poisson branching survival fraction solves `rho=1-exp(-c rho)` and is positive exactly for `c>1`. | Infinite branching-process model. | Note 144 |
| SEM-1405 | Classical fact | Sparse Erdos-Renyi graphs have no linear giant for `c<=1` and a unique asymptotic giant fraction `rho` for `c>1` away from the critical boundary. | `G(n,c/n)` asymptotic regime. | Note 144; Erdos-Renyi 1960 |
| SEM-1406 | Conditioning boundary | Largest-component-root BFS, ordinary-root BFS, and survival-conditioned BFS have different frontier distributions. | Exchangeable labels and declared root rule. | Note 144 |
| SEM-1407 | Inference | In the supercritical limit an unconditioned random root has expected component fraction approximately `rho^2`. | Root enters the giant with probability `rho`, whose fraction is `rho`; small components are negligible after normalization. | Note 144 |
| SEM-1408 | Collision boundary | Tree-like expansion fails through earlier-ball hits, same-layer edges, multiple next-layer parents, and finite-population depletion. | Frozen finite graph BFS. | Note 144 |
| SEM-1409 | Configuration-model fact | The branching mean reached through an edge is `E[D(D-1)]/E[D]`, not generally `E[D]`. | Degree-distribution regularity and configuration-model scope. | Note 144; Molloy-Reed 1995 |
| SEM-1410 | Criticality boundary | Critical largest components have `n^(2/3)` scale and high variability rather than a stable positive fraction. | Classical Erdos-Renyi critical window. | Note 144; Erdos-Renyi 1960 |
| SEM-1411 | Validated observation | At `n=2000`, mean largest fractions across 20 seeds were 0.0207, 0.0689, 0.2996, and 0.9797 for `c=0.8,1.0,1.2,4.0`. | Declared xorshift sampler and finite sample set. | Note 144; REF-038 |
| SEM-1412 | Validated observation | The representative `c=4` giant wave peaked at 765 vertices and outward occurrences per new state peaked near 1.88. | One retained seed; not an ensemble law or performance claim. | Note 144; REF-038 |
| SEM-1413 | Distributed rule | Implicit random adjacency must use stable canonical-pair decisions across owners; schedule-dependent RNG consumption can define inconsistent graphs. | Exact undirected distributed traversal. | Note 144 |
| SEM-1414 | Measurement rule | Random-graph studies must retain seeds, root conditioning, replicate variability, graph-generation cost, and BFS work separately. | Ensemble evidence contract. | Note 144 |
| SEM-1415 | Reporting rule | Vertex/edge work, collision classes, owner skew, routing, synchronization, and end-to-end time are separate random variables and claims. | Semantic and physical work decomposition. | Note 144 |
| SEM-1416 | Definition boundary | Degree regularity, distance regularity, and regular group action are distinct properties. | Terminology fixed before transfer. | Note 145 |
| SEM-1417 | Pairing fact | A uniform stub pairing conditioned on simplicity induces the uniform labeled simple regular-graph distribution. | Every simple `r`-regular graph has the same `(r!)^n` pairing multiplicity. | Note 145; Wormald 1999 |
| SEM-1418 | Classical fact | For fixed `r`, pairing simplicity probability tends to `exp(-(r^2-1)/4)`. | Fixed-degree asymptotic pairing model. | Note 145; Bollobas 1980 |
| SEM-1419 | Proven | Every finite simple `r`-regular BFS satisfies `|F_d|<=r(r-1)^(d-1)` for `d>=1`. | Root degree and one mandatory inward edge per nonroot discovery. | Note 145 |
| SEM-1420 | Local-limit fact | Uniform fixed-degree random regular graphs converge locally to the infinite `r`-regular tree. | Fixed radius and fixed `r` as `n` grows. | Note 145; Wormald 1999 |
| SEM-1421 | Branching boundary | Random regular exploration has root branching `r` and local nonroot branching `r-1`, unlike Poisson Erdos-Renyi's mean-`c` excess law. | Early tree-like regime. | Notes 144 and 145 |
| SEM-1422 | Rejected implication | Equal degree does not imply equal inward/same/outward counts within one BFS layer. | Representative 4-regular depth-four ranges `[1,2]`, `[0,1]`, `[2,3]`. | Note 145; REF-039 |
| SEM-1423 | Classical fact | Uniform random fixed `r>=3` regular graphs are connected with probability tending to one. | Fixed `r`; excludes degrees zero, one, and two. | Note 145; Wormald 1999 |
| SEM-1424 | Validated observation | All forty retained `n=2000` degree-three/four samples were exactly regular and connected. | Declared deterministic PRNG and pairing rejection. | Note 145; REF-039 |
| SEM-1425 | Validated observation | Representative profiles first fell below tree bounds at 46 versus 48 for `r=3` and 106 versus 108 for `r=4`. | One seed per degree; collision-onset case studies. | Note 145; REF-039 |
| SEM-1426 | Validated observation | Mean rejection attempts were 6.45 for `r=3` and 46.80 for `r=4`, with ranges `[1,37]` and `[2,134]`. | Twenty deterministic samples per degree; not limiting estimates. | Note 145; REF-039 |
| SEM-1427 | Sampling boundary | Removing loops or merging parallel edges after pairing is not equivalent to conditioning on a simple regular graph. | Degree and distribution contracts differ. | Note 145 |
| SEM-1428 | Measurement rule | Large outward-per-new ratios in tiny tail layers require absolute counts and cannot alone characterize collision pressure. | Ratio denominator may be very small. | Note 145 |
| SEM-1429 | Calibration boundary | Fixed degree removes adjacency-count imbalance but not radial outcomes, memory locality, owner traffic, or contention. | Logical and physical regularity separated. | Note 145 |
| SEM-1430 | Reporting rule | Pairing attempts, graph construction, BFS edge classes, accepted states, routing, synchronization, and end-to-end time are separate claims. | Generation and traversal work decomposition. | Note 145 |
| SEM-1431 | Model boundary | An SBM BFS traverses one frozen conditional edge sample; resampling adjacency on query defines a different stochastic process. | Static exact BFS. | Note 146 |
| SEM-1432 | Qualified fact | Sparse SBM exploration is locally approximated by a multitype Poisson branching process with mean matrix `M_ab=alpha_b c_ab`. | Before finite depletion/collisions; declared type proportions. | Note 146; Bollobas-Janson-Riordan 2007 |
| SEM-1433 | Convention rule | Expected typed frontiers use `f_(d+1) approximately f_d M` for row vectors and `M^T f_d` for columns. | Orientation must be declared. | Note 146 |
| SEM-1434 | Qualified fact | Under irreducibility, the Perron eigenvalue controls the giant/survival threshold; reducible kernels require component-wise interpretation. | Sparse inhomogeneous random-graph assumptions. | Note 146; Bollobas-Janson-Riordan 2007 |
| SEM-1435 | Inference | In the symmetric two-block model, `(within-across)/2` controls persistence or sign alternation of expected type contrast while `(within+across)/2` controls total early growth. | Equal block proportions and symmetric coefficients. | Note 146 |
| SEM-1436 | Rejected implication | Equal mean degree and equal Perron growth do not determine the typed BFS frontier. | Four retained two-block cases all have mean degree and Perron eigenvalue four. | Note 146; REF-040 |
| SEM-1437 | Reducibility boundary | Spectral radius above one does not imply one unique global giant when the type kernel is reducible. | `across=0` yields disconnected block classes. | Note 146; REF-040 |
| SEM-1438 | Validated observation | Mean largest fractions were 0.4912 for segregated and about 0.979 for all three irreducible cases. | `n=2000`, 20 declared seeds per case. | Note 146; REF-040 |
| SEM-1439 | Validated observation | The disassortative representative frontier alternated type dominance across successive layers. | One retained seed; qualitative case study, not ensemble law. | Note 146; REF-040 |
| SEM-1440 | Validated observation | Block-owner remote fractions were 0.0000, 0.0626, 0.4987, and 0.9376 from segregated through disassortative cases; striped ownership stayed near one half. | Eighty retained finite samples. | Note 146; REF-040 |
| SEM-1441 | Rejected universal | Community/block ownership does not universally minimize communication. | It helps assortative cases and hurts disassortative cases. | Note 146; REF-040 |
| SEM-1442 | Calibration boundary | A zero-cut partition can leave owners idle when a single-source reachable component lies wholly on one owner. | Segregated two-block case. | Note 146; REF-040 |
| SEM-1443 | Separation rule | Inferred block labels can alter partition traffic without altering BFS distances in the original frozen graph. | Exact traversal identity independent of analysis labels. | Note 146 |
| SEM-1444 | Reporting rule | Typed/owner frontiers, routing matrices, collision classes, load skew, synchronization, and end-to-end time are separate claims. | Distributed SBM observation contract. | Note 146 |
| SEM-1445 | Model boundary | A direct configuration-model pairing is a multigraph; conditioning on simplicity or collapsing occurrences defines a different distribution/degree contract. | Frozen stub pairing. | Note 147 |
| SEM-1446 | Proven | An edge endpoint has size-biased degree law `P(D*=k)=k p_k/E[D]`. | Uniform random stub pairing and finite positive mean degree. | Note 147 |
| SEM-1447 | Proven | `E[D*]=E[D^2]/E[D]=E[D]+Var(D)/E[D]`. | Finite second moment. | Note 147 |
| SEM-1448 | Qualified fact | Local post-root configuration-model offspring has law `D*-1` and mean `nu=E[D(D-1)]/E[D]`. | Before collisions/depletion; locally tree-like regularity assumptions. | Note 147; Newman-Strogatz-Watts 2001 |
| SEM-1449 | Branching boundary | The root uses ordinary degree `D`; later edge-reached vertices use the size-biased excess law. | Uniform unconditioned root. | Note 147 |
| SEM-1450 | Classical fact | The Molloy-Reed giant criterion is the sign of `E[D(D-2)]=E[D](nu-1)` under its stated degree-sequence conditions. | Asymptotic theorem scope. | Note 147; Molloy-Reed 1995 |
| SEM-1451 | Rejected implication | Equal average degree does not determine local BFS branching, giant coverage, or adjacency work. | Three mean-four degree multisets. | Note 147; REF-041 |
| SEM-1452 | Validated observation | At mean degree four, retained excess means were 3.00, 4.00, and 5.25 for regular, half-2/6, and half-1/7 sequences. | Exact declared degree multisets. | Note 147; REF-041 |
| SEM-1453 | Validated observation | Mean largest fractions were 1.0000, 0.9999, and 0.9380 despite increasing excess mean. | Twenty deterministic pairings per distribution at `n=2000`. | Note 147; REF-041 |
| SEM-1454 | Conditioning boundary | The half-1/7 root entered the giant in 18/20 samples, producing mean root component fraction 0.8435; this is not a precise survival estimate. | Shuffled degree labels and fixed root zero. | Note 147; REF-041 |
| SEM-1455 | Validated observation | Representative half-1/7 frontier mean degree fell from hub-rich 7.00 early layers to 1.10 and 1.00 in the tail. | One retained finite pairing. | Note 147; REF-041 |
| SEM-1456 | Work separation | Frontier vertex count, scanned degree mass, edge occurrences, and distinct new states are different quantities. | Adjacency-list BFS. | Note 147 |
| SEM-1457 | Calibration boundary | High excess mean can coexist with lower giant coverage when degree-one/zero mass remains outside the surviving component. | Degree-distribution giant equations. | Note 147 |
| SEM-1458 | GPU inference | Equal vertex counts across owners need not balance edge occurrences when frontier degree composition varies. | Static vertex ownership; no throughput claim. | Note 147 |
| SEM-1459 | Reporting rule | Degree histograms, maximum/sum degree, radial occurrence classes, accepted states, routing, synchronization, and end-to-end time are separate claims. | Heterogeneous-degree traversal observation. | Note 147 |
| SEM-1460 | Definition | Forward BFS computes `R+(s)` and transpose BFS computes `R-(s)` in a fixed digraph. | Complete exact outgoing adjacency in the selected orientation. | Notes 05, 84, and 148 |
| SEM-1461 | Proven | `SCC(s)=R+(s) intersection R-(s)`. | Complete forward and transpose reachability. | Notes 84 and 148 |
| SEM-1462 | Model boundary | Opposite arcs in `D(n,c/n)` are independent events; transposition preserves the ensemble law, not one sample's frontier. | Symmetric independent ordered-pair model. | Note 148 |
| SEM-1463 | Qualified fact | Early forward and reverse exploration are each approximated by Poisson-`c` branching. | Sparse directed ER, local pre-collision scope. | Note 148 |
| SEM-1464 | Classical fact | For `c>1`, directed ER has asymptotic GIN and GOUT fractions `rho` and GSCC fraction `rho^2`, where `rho=1-exp(-c rho)`. | Independent directed arcs and asymptotic regime. | Note 148; Karp 1990 |
| SEM-1465 | Conditioning boundary | A random root has giant forward reach only when it lies in GIN; its unconditioned normalized expectation is approximately `rho^2`. | Supercritical directed ER. | Note 148 |
| SEM-1466 | Conditioning boundary | A random root has a giant SCC only when it lies in GSCC; unconditioned normalized root-SCC expectation is approximately `rho^4`. | Supercritical directed ER. | Note 148 |
| SEM-1467 | Terminology boundary | Reachability to/from the largest finite SCC at `c<=1` is not a giant IN/OUT component. | Subcritical and critical finite samples. | Note 148; REF-042 |
| SEM-1468 | Validated observation | At `c=4`, mean largest-SCC, core-reverse, and core-forward fractions were 0.9611, 0.9804, and 0.9804. | Twenty `n=2000` deterministic samples. | Note 148; REF-042 |
| SEM-1469 | Validated observation | At `c=4`, root zero reached the largest core in 18/20 samples, was reachable from it in 20/20, and belonged to it in 18/20. | Shuffled ensemble through fixed label; finite conditioning evidence. | Note 148; REF-042 |
| SEM-1470 | Validated observation | One `c=4` sample had forward layers peaking at 836 and reverse layers peaking at 754 at different depths. | One retained root/sample; no ensemble or performance claim. | Note 148; REF-042 |
| SEM-1471 | Rejected implication | Similar total forward and reverse reach does not imply similar frontier profiles or synchronization work. | Representative `c=4` transpose pair. | Note 148; REF-042 |
| SEM-1472 | SCC boundary | Two root traversals identify one root SCC, not the complete SCC partition. | Full graph may contain other SCCs. | Notes 84 and 148 |
| SEM-1473 | Storage boundary | Reverse BFS requires transpose-neighbor access; outgoing adjacency alone does not supply it for free. | Directed graph representation. | Note 148 |
| SEM-1474 | Reporting rule | Forward/transpose storage, frontier waves, scans, routing, visited outcomes, intersection, synchronization, and end-to-end time are separate claims. | Directed distributed traversal. | Note 148 |
| SEM-1475 | Validation rule | Iterative DFS finish order cannot be inferred from eager sibling marking; an SCC measurement oracle must preserve true nested finish times. | Kosaraju first pass; initial REF-042 SCC values were rejected and recomputed. | Note 148; REF-042 |

| SEM-1506 | Reporting rule | Base/shortcut construction, changed metric, frontier profile, benefited states, owner onset, routing, synchronization, and end-to-end time are separate claims. | Small-world traversal. | Note 150 |

| SEM-1512 | Rejected implication | A high-degree frontier need not produce a proportionally large next frontier. | Core edges may converge on already visited or multiply proposed vertices. | Note 151 |

| SEM-1518 | Reporting rule | Model construction, birth/degree composition, candidate collisions, core-entry depth, ownership, routing, synchronization, and end-to-end time are separate claims. | Preferential-attachment traversal. | Note 151 |

| SEM-1520 | Proved fact | Rerooting a tree from `q` to `r` reverses exactly the edges on the unique `q-r` path. | Parent edges oriented away from each root. | Note 152 |
| SEM-1521 | Proved fact | The number of parent-edge reversals under tree rerooting equals `dist(q,r)`. | Same tree and roots. | Note 152 |

| SEM-1523 | Proved fact | Tree BFS obeys `|F_1|=deg(r)` and `|F_(d+1)|=sum_(v in F_d)(deg(v)-1)` for `d>=1`. | Finite or locally finite undirected tree. | Note 152 |

| SEM-1525 | Correction | Collision-heavy hub-core expansion is not a universal preferential-attachment property. | Absent in the simple `m=1` tree; possible once cycles exist. | Notes 151-152 |

| SEM-1529 | Semantic separation | Frozen BFS is insensitive to birth orientation; birth time matters through topology, layout, or ownership. | Ordinary exact BFS over the frozen graph. | Note 152 |
| SEM-1530 | Reporting rule | Growth parent, query-root BFS parent, frontier degree mass, owner edge cut, and duplicate class are separate objects. | Growing-tree traversal. | Note 152 |
| SEM-1531 | Structural fact | A connected simple unicyclic graph is one cycle with one rooted tree attached at each cycle vertex. | Finite connected simple undirected graph with `m=n`. | Note 153 |
| SEM-1532 | Qualified fact | Outside the source attachment tree, distance equals source-to-cycle-gate distance plus minimum cyclic distance plus target attachment depth; inside the source tree use its tree distance. | Connected simple unicyclic graph; the source may lie off-cycle. | Corrected note 153; source-leaf self-distance counterexample in audit E09 |
| SEM-1533 | Proved fact | An odd unique cycle manifests as exactly one same-layer cycle edge at its farthest pair. | Cycle length `2k+1`; arbitrary source. | Notes 31, 82, and 153 |
| SEM-1534 | Proved fact | An even unique cycle manifests as exactly one antipodal cycle vertex with two shortest predecessors. | Cycle length `2k`; arbitrary source. | Notes 82 and 153 |
