| SEM-1537 | Rejected implication | Tiny duplicate count does not justify removing visited from a general traversal. | One cycle already permits recirculation under parent-only exclusion. | Note 153 |
| SEM-1538 | Qualified recurrence | An odd cycle of length 2k+1 adds two same-layer scan occurrences at depth h+k. An even cycle of length 2k merges two antipode proposals from depth h+k-1, then the antipode expansion at h+k excludes both old cycle neighbors. | Complete exact BFS in a simple unicyclic graph; h is source-to-cycle distance; antipode outward tree degree is degree minus two, not degree minus one. | Corrected notes 152-153 |

| SEM-1545 | Proved fact | Cactus distance is the sum of bridge costs and minimum cyclic-arc distances along the unique block-tree route. | Finite connected simple cactus. | Note 154 |

| SEM-1550 | Qualified fact | Cactus cycle events are additive in cycle-space rank but multiplicative in geodesic count when even antipodal crossings compose. | Edge-disjoint cycle blocks. | Note 154 |
| SEM-1551 | Rejected implication | Block-tree decomposability does not give a scalar product formula for the global BFS frontier profile. | Many branches and cycles can be active at one depth. | Note 154 |

| SEM-1554 | Rejected implication | Few articulation cuts do not imply balanced per-level owner work. | Attached subtrees and cycle waves may remain owner-local. | Notes 51 and 154 |

| SEM-1628 | Counterexample | The three-point `S_3` fixture has forward support counts `2,3,2` and reverse counts `2,2,3`. | `S={(12),(13),(123)}`. | Notes 158-159 |
| SEM-1629 | Semantic separation | Backward exactness requires distance-to-target labels; smaller-side expansion is only a work policy. | Bidirectional BFS. | Notes 05, 08, 56, and 159 |
| SEM-1630 | Rejected implication | Smaller state frontier guarantees smaller occurrence, support-arc, or routed work. | Direction/state-dependent alias profiles. | Note 159 |
| SEM-1631 | Replay boundary | A reverse edge labeled `s^-1` must be converted to original forward label `s` when stitching a path. | Right-action replay. | Notes 57 and 159 |
| SEM-1632 | Output boundary | Reverse endpoint dedup may discard labels required by a labeled DAG or path count. | Nonfree action and rich output. | Notes 57, 64, and 159 |
| SEM-1633 | Distributed inference | One owner function can induce different forward and reverse routing matrices on the same state orbit. | Asymmetric support occurrences. | Note 159 |
| SEM-1634 | Reporting rule | Forward/reverse generator sets, support degrees, aliases, routing, meeting metadata, and replay-label inversion are separate claims. | Bidirectional Schreier BFS. | Note 159 |
| SEM-1635 | Definition | `l(x)=|S intersect Stab(x)|`, `u(x)=|{Ks:s in S}|`, and `q(x)=u(x)-1_[l(x)>0]` count loop occurrences, all support endpoints, and nonloop support endpoints. | Fixed right action and generator collection. | Notes 158 and 160 |
| SEM-1636 | Proved fact | Per-parent nonloop alias excess is `r(x)=|S|-l(x)-q(x)`. | Labeled occurrence collection projected to nonloop support arcs. | Note 160 |
| SEM-1637 | Proved fact | Level raw occurrences split as `G_d=L_d+R_d+P_d`, where `P_d` is distinct nonloop parent-child support arcs. | Complete frontier expansion. | Note 160 |
| SEM-1638 | Proved fact | Distinct nonloop support arcs split as `P_d=V_d+C_d` into visited-ball and next-layer arcs. | Exact BFS layers. | Note 160 |
| SEM-1639 | Proved fact | Cross-parent next-state excess is `D_d=C_d-|F_(d+1)|`. | At least one support parent per next state. | Note 160 |
| SEM-1640 | Proved fact | The stabilizer-aware waterfall is `G_d=L_d+R_d+V_d+D_d+|F_(d+1)|`. | Finite complete level and vertex-frontier identity. | Note 160 |
| SEM-1641 | Semantic separation | Loops, same-parent nonloop aliases, visited support arcs, and cross-parent convergence are distinct causes of nonaccepting vertex occurrences. | Note-160 definitions. | Note 160 |
| SEM-1642 | Counterexample refinement | In the `S_3` fixture, states one and three have equal total support endpoints but nonloop support-arc counts two and one. | Loop versus alias composition differs. | Notes 158 and 160 |
| SEM-1643 | Cayley specialization | A free action with distinct nonidentity generators has `L_d=R_d=0` but may retain visited hits and cross-parent convergence. | Regular Cayley action. | Notes 157-158 and 160 |
| SEM-1644 | Output boundary | A class absent from the next vertex frontier may still be required as loop, label, cycle, predecessor, or path-count metadata. | Declared rich output. | Notes 57, 64, and 160 |
| SEM-1645 | Rejected implication | The semantic waterfall order prescribes the fastest physical filtering order. | Indexing, locality, branching, storage, and synchronization costs remain. | Note 160 |
| SEM-1646 | Distributed boundary | Alias collapse before routing, visited filtering before routing, and owner-side cross-parent convergence require different authority and metadata assumptions. | Owner-computes model. | Notes 51-52 and 160 |
| SEM-1647 | Rejected implication | Equal raw generator occurrences per owner imply equal support, unvisited, accepted, byte, or transform work. | Stabilizer and state-cost profiles may differ. | Note 160 |
| SEM-1648 | Instrumentation rule | Owner matrices should be retained at raw-occurrence, support-arc, unvisited-arc, and accepted-state boundaries. | Multi-owner implicit BFS. | Note 160 |
| SEM-1649 | Performance boundary | Composition ratios are not speedup claims until work removal and overhead are measured. | Existing implementation required. | Notes 29, 47, and 160 |
| SEM-1650 | Reporting rule | Generator time, loops, parent aliases, visited arcs, parent convergence, accepted states, bytes, retries, metadata, and capacity are separate claims. | Stabilizer-aware traversal. | Note 160 |
| SEM-1651 | Definition | Directed support arcs from `F_d` split into `P_d` arcs to `F_(d+1)` and `Q_d` arcs to visited ball `B_d`. | Complete finite reachable support graph. | Notes 157 and 161 |
| SEM-1652 | Proved fact | Directed support arc count satisfies `m=sum_d(P_d+Q_d)`. | Exact BFS endpoint layers. | Note 161 |
| SEM-1653 | Proved fact | Directed arborescence surplus satisfies `m-(n-1)=sum_d Q_d+sum_d(P_d-|F_(d+1)|)`. | One predecessor selected per nonroot state. | Note 161 |
| SEM-1654 | Proved fact | A complete claim-before-enqueue support traversal has exactly `m-(n-1)` nonaccepting arc attempts. | Each support arc scanned once. | Notes 74 and 161 |
| SEM-1655 | Definition | `Q_(d,k)` and lag `d-k` refine visited arcs by their endpoint BFS layer. | `0<=k<=d`. | Note 161 |
| SEM-1656 | Rejected implication | A directed arc to the same or an earlier BFS layer necessarily lies on a directed cycle. | Return reachability is additionally required. | Notes 84 and 161 |
| SEM-1657 | Counterexample | A complete acyclic ordered DAG has quadratic `m-n+1`, entirely as same-layer arcs under BFS from its minimum vertex. | Arcs `i->j` for `i<j`. | Note 161 |
| SEM-1658 | Counterexample | Directed diamond and directed cycle both have surplus one but place it in predecessor excess versus a return arc. | Exact small support graphs. | Note 161 |
| SEM-1659 | Rejected implication | `m-n+1` counts directed cycles. | It is arc surplus over a BFS arborescence and can be large in a DAG. | Note 161 |
| SEM-1660 | Qualified fact | An arc lies on a directed cycle exactly when its head can reach its tail. | Directed support graph. | Notes 84 and 161 |
| SEM-1661 | Condensation boundary | SCC contraction removes directed cycles but does not force every condensation arc to advance one BFS layer. | Condensation is a DAG with possible deep-to-shallow arcs. | Notes 84 and 161 |
| SEM-1662 | Output boundary | Next-layer predecessor excess is required by a shortest-path DAG, while visited-ball arcs are not shortest predecessors. | Unit directed distances. | Notes 11 and 161 |
| SEM-1663 | Distributed boundary | Visited-ball rejection and new-state parent convergence require different membership and owner coordination. | Exact owner-authoritative BFS. | Notes 51-52 and 161 |
| SEM-1664 | Rejected implication | Back-depth lag alone predicts communication locality or bytes. | Owner mapping and representation remain necessary. | Note 161 |
| SEM-1665 | Direction boundary | Forward and reverse traversals can have different radial surplus distributions even on the same arc set. | Transpose layers differ. | Notes 75, 159, and 161 |
| SEM-1666 | Reporting rule | Support arcs, back-depth spectrum, predecessor excess, SCC/cycle status, routing, and retained output arcs are separate claims. | Directed BFS. | Note 161 |
| SEM-1667 | Definition | A completed depth-`d` prefix has retired every logical successor obligation from `F_0,...,F_d` and constructed exact `B_(d+1)`. | Unit-cost exact BFS. | Note 162 |
| SEM-1668 | Proved fact | Prefix nonaccepting occurrences equal `sum_(i=0)^d T_i-(|B_(d+1)|-1)`. | One accepted claim per nonroot state in the exact ball. | Notes 157 and 162 |
| SEM-1669 | Proved fact | Exact radius-`R` membership requires complete expansion through `F_(R-1)`, not expansion of `F_R`. | Complete successor enumeration and identity. | Notes 42 and 162 |
| SEM-1670 | Rejected implication | Arithmetic balance for a processed subset `U` of a frontier proves complete `F_(d+1)`. | Unexpanded parents may add states or predecessors. | Note 162 |
| SEM-1671 | Qualified fact | With exact `B_d`, generation of target from `F_d` proves scalar distance `d+1`. | Unit costs and replay-valid occurrence. | Notes 08, 57, and 162 |
| SEM-1672 | Output boundary | Mid-layer target discovery need not finalize canonical parent, all predecessors, path count, all connectors, or complete next frontier. | Richer outputs require equal-depth closure. | Notes 57 and 162 |
| SEM-1673 | Negative-result boundary | Absence after a partial layer proves no `d+1` lower bound; absence after complete `F_d` proves distance greater than `d+1` or infinity. | Exact completed boundary. | Notes 42 and 162 |
| SEM-1674 | Rejected implication | Early-stop work is invariant because final frontier sets are invariant. | Parent order, batching, target check, and cancellation change partial work. | Notes 08 and 162 |
| SEM-1675 | Capacity boundary | A fitting final frontier does not prove intermediate candidate/routing capacity, and logical occurrence count need not equal resident peak. | Streaming and fixed buffers. | Notes 29, 30, and 162 |
| SEM-1676 | Failure boundary | Overflow or loss before prefix closure yields incomplete/unknown rather than a smaller exact frontier. | No silent loss permitted. | Notes 09, 30, 42, and 162 |
| SEM-1677 | Distributed boundary | Local rank completion does not prove a completed layer while messages, owners, retries, kernels, or publications remain unfinished. | Global consistent cut. | Notes 30, 52, 56, and 162 |
| SEM-1678 | Qualified fact | Completed-level waterfall identities may be summed over an exact prefix; partial-level counters must remain labeled partial. | Same logical occurrence contract. | Notes 160 and 162 |
| SEM-1679 | Reporting boundary | Completed semantic prefix totals and partial/cancelled latency work are different measurement objects. | Early-stop execution. | Note 162 |
| SEM-1680 | Bidirectional boundary | One intersection does not replace unfinished-depth lower bounds for partial bidirectional stopping. | Possible unseen connectors on either side. | Notes 56 and 162 |
| SEM-1681 | Applicability boundary | Tentative asynchronous relaxation, beam pruning, probabilistic loss, or incomplete successors are not exact BFS prefixes without their own proof. | Different algorithm/outcome semantics. | Notes 09, 18, 24, and 162 |
| SEM-1682 | Reporting rule | Completed depths, partial parent subset, in-flight work, accepted states, output closure, capacity, and stop reason are separate claims. | Bounded/early-stop BFS. | Note 162 |
| SEM-1683 | Counterexample | Distinct sets `{0,3}` and `{1,2}` have equal cardinality, sum, and xor. | Exact integer state fixture. | Note 163 |
| SEM-1684 | Rejected implication | Matching conservation counters prove exact frontier correctness. | Balanced semantic errors can preserve all declared totals. | Note 163 |
| SEM-1685 | Qualified fact | A failed exact conservation identity proves a bug or violated accounting assumption. | The identity and semantic cut are correctly specified. | Notes 160, 162, and 163 |
| SEM-1686 | Evidence boundary | Matching finite fingerprints are probabilistic evidence unless injectivity is proved on the stated domain. | Collision-resolving equality remains separate. | Notes 28 and 163 |
| SEM-1687 | Evidence boundary | Replay-valid paths prove positive reachability witnesses, not successor completeness or frontier completeness. | Exact implicit graph search. | Notes 28, 55, and 163 |
| SEM-1688 | Exact-comparison rule | Exact frontier equality requires canonical state identity and collision-resolving set comparison. | Tractable reference domain. | Notes 28 and 163 |
| SEM-1689 | Independence boundary | CPU/GPU or worker-count parity can preserve a shared move, encoder, or omission bug. | Implementations share dependencies. | Notes 55 and 163 |
| SEM-1690 | Distributed boundary | Global reductions can hide balanced losses and extras across owners. | Per-owner matrices improve localization but not proof. | Note 163 |
| SEM-1691 | Retry boundary | Stable logical identities are required to distinguish graph occurrences from transport retries. | Distributed exact BFS. | Notes 56, 160, and 163 |
| SEM-1692 | Verification ladder | Counters, matrices, fingerprints, replay, exact set equality, and exhaustive independent oracles provide progressively stronger complementary evidence. | Scope remains explicit at every rung. | Note 163 |
| SEM-1693 | Reporting rule | Largest exhaustively validated domain and largest performance run are separate claims. | BFS experiments at any device count. | Note 163 |
| SEM-1694 | Taxonomy | Exact unit-distance execution can use completed-layer first claim, nondecreasing-key settlement, or fair label correction. | Each contract retains its own proof obligations. | Note 164 |
| SEM-1695 | Qualified fact | Physical order inside an exact frontier is irrelevant to scalar distances. | Every current-layer obligation closes before deeper expansion. | Notes 03 and 164 |
| SEM-1696 | Qualified fact | Strict FIFO unit-edge BFS is a compact nondecreasing-key label-setting schedule. | Complete successors and correct stale-copy handling. | Notes 03, 74, and 164 |
| SEM-1697 | Qualified fact | Fair arbitrary-order relaxation can converge to BFS distances. | Atomic minima, reactivation, no loss, real witnesses, and global quiescence. | Notes 18 and 164 |
| SEM-1698 | Confluence boundary | Final distances may be schedule-confluent while parents, transient frontiers, messages, and work are not. | Label-correcting execution. | Note 164 |
| SEM-1699 | Rejected hybrid | Arbitrary scheduling plus irrevocable first claim is exact BFS. | A longer route can win before a shorter delayed route. | Notes 03, 18, and 164 |
| SEM-1700 | Rejected hybrid | Atomic minimum updates suffice without reactivation. | Improved descendants can remain stale. | Notes 18 and 164 |
| SEM-1701 | Event boundary | Discovery, claim, activation, settlement, and finalization have contract-dependent meanings. | Parallel and distributed BFS. | Notes 57, 74, and 164 |
| SEM-1702 | Target boundary | First target discovery is final under a closed-layer premise, but only an upper bound under arbitrary-order correction. | Unit-cost exact search. | Notes 18, 57, 162, and 164 |
| SEM-1703 | Distributed boundary | Layer closure, global minimum, and global quiescence are distinct multi-worker proof obligations. | Contracts L, K, and R respectively. | Note 164 |
| SEM-1704 | Reporting rule | Completed-depth work, stale settlements, and corrective reactivation work belong to different execution contracts. | Cross-implementation comparisons. | Note 164 |
| SEM-1705 | Definition | A completed level has distinct semantic coordinates for frontier states, logical occurrences, support arcs, next support arcs, and accepted states. | Exact vertex-frontier BFS. | Notes 160 and 165 |
| SEM-1706 | Proved ordering | `0 <= n_d <= c_d <= p_d <= g_d`. | Declared occurrence/support contracts. | Notes 157, 160, and 165 |
| SEM-1707 | Conversion boundary | Logical occurrences are not universally equal to threads, records, probes, atomics, transactions, or bytes. | Representation- and protocol-dependent execution. | Note 165 |
| SEM-1708 | Qualified metric | Acceptance, support, execution, probe, routing, and byte ratios require named numerators and denominators. | Per-level measurement. | Note 165 |
| SEM-1709 | Terminal boundary | Cost per accepted state is undefined when a terminal expansion has positive work and zero accepted states. | `n_d=0`. | Note 165 |
| SEM-1710 | Rejected implication | A semantic duplicate ratio predicts physical work saved. | Duplicate meeting scope and mechanism are unspecified. | Notes 07, 160, and 165 |
| SEM-1711 | Work-span boundary | Total work, maximum owner work, dependency depth, and elapsed time are distinct quantities. | Parallel/distributed execution. | Notes 47 and 165 |
| SEM-1712 | Rejected implication | Balanced logical occurrence counts prove balanced bytes, contention, or level time. | Record widths, locality, and skew can differ. | Notes 51 and 165 |
| SEM-1713 | Communication boundary | Remote occurrences, support arcs, candidate states, accepted states, routed records, payload bytes, and protocol bytes are distinct. | Owner-partitioned BFS. | Notes 51, 160, and 165 |
| SEM-1714 | Rejected implication | Every remote logical arc must transmit a full state. | Replication, compact identity, provenance, or recomputation alter representation. | Note 165 |
| SEM-1715 | Rejected implication | Fewer routed records always means fewer wire bytes. | Surviving records and protocol metadata can be wider. | Note 165 |
| SEM-1716 | Multi-device rule | One- and multi-GPU runs should share semantic coordinates and differ in their measured physical vectors. | Same graph and output contract. | Note 165 |
| SEM-1717 | Reporting rule | Graph geometry, physical realization, and elapsed-time conversion are three separate evidence layers. | BFS performance study. | Notes 54 and 165 |
| SEM-1718 | Definition | Strong scaling holds one BFS workload contract fixed while changing GPU count. | Same graph, source, direction, stopping, output, and exactness semantics. | Note 166 |
| SEM-1719 | Measurement rule | `S_p=T_1/T_p` and `E_p=S_p/p` require a feasible matching one-GPU baseline. | Fixed-work end-to-end traversal. | Note 166 |
| SEM-1720 | Capacity boundary | A larger exact feasible workload is capacity evidence, not fixed-work speedup. | Memory and correctness accounting supplied. | Notes 07 and 166 |
| SEM-1721 | Rejected implication | Aggregate nominal GPU memory equals usable exact BFS capacity. | Replication, scratch, buffers, skew, and headroom consume capacity. | Notes 36, 51, and 166 |
| SEM-1722 | Definition | Independent-query throughput is completed separate BFS instances per elapsed time. | Separate visited sets and outputs. | Note 166 |
| SEM-1723 | Rejected equivalence | A batch of independent BFS queries is multisource BFS. | Multisource BFS computes minimum distance over sources and merges waves. | Notes 13 and 166 |
| SEM-1724 | Weak-scaling boundary | Fixed nominal vertices per GPU need not preserve reachable work, frontier profiles, duplicates, or communication. | Growing BFS graph family. | Note 166 |
| SEM-1725 | Regime boundary | Weak scaling and capacity scaling both change workload but answer different questions. | Declared growth versus maximum feasibility. | Note 166 |
| SEM-1726 | Profile boundary | Whole-run strong scaling can hide underfilled tails, scalable middle levels, and termination-dominated late levels. | Per-level exact trace. | Notes 47 and 166 |
| SEM-1727 | Qualified observation | Measured superlinear speedup can arise from a changed memory or execution regime. | Semantic workload equivalence must be verified. | Note 166 |
| SEM-1728 | Trade-off boundary | Latency, strong-scaling efficiency, independent-query throughput, and maximum capacity are separate outcomes. | GPU BFS evaluation. | Note 166 |
| SEM-1729 | Evidence boundary | Isolated kernels and simulated wire traffic do not establish real multi-GPU traversal scaling. | End-to-end topology-aware measurement required. | Notes 02, 07, and 166 |
| SEM-1730 | Reporting rule | Fixed-work, weak-growth, capacity-limit, and query-throughput experiments require separate rows. | Scaling study. | Note 166 |
| SEM-1731 | Proved Cayley locality | Under right multiplication and left-coset blocks `gH`, an `s`-edge is block-local exactly when `s in H`. | Cayley vertices and one block per coset. | Note 167 |
| SEM-1732 | Proved occurrence fractions | Local and remote labeled-occurrence fractions are `|S intersect H|/|S|` and `|S minus H|/|S|`. | Same Cayley coset contract. | Note 167 |
| SEM-1733 | Action-side boundary | Left multiplication uses right-coset blocks `Hg` for the analogous locality law. | Consistent action/coset convention. | Notes 16 and 167 |
| SEM-1734 | Rejected implication | Equal-size Cayley cosets imply balanced BFS frontiers. | Frontier intersections with owner blocks may be skewed by depth. | Note 167 |
| SEM-1735 | Normality boundary | Normality makes quotient transitions representative-independent but is not required for subgroup-label locality. | Right Cayley action on left-coset blocks `gH`. | Note 167 |
| SEM-1736 | Coalescing boundary | Mapping several cosets to one GPU preserves all subgroup-label locality and can make additional outside edges device-local. | Explicit coset-to-GPU map. | Note 167 |
| SEM-1737 | Schreier ownership | Right `H`-orbits of states `K\G` correspond to double cosets `K\G/H`. | Right Schreier action. | Notes 158 and 167 |
| SEM-1738 | Proved Schreier orbit size | `|(Kg)H|=[H:H intersect g^-1Kg]`. | Finite group action. | Notes 158 and 167 |
| SEM-1739 | Cayley-Schreier boundary | Subgroup-local Schreier owner blocks can have unequal capacity because stabilizers vary. | Nonfree action. | Note 167 |
| SEM-1740 | Rejected transfer | An outside generator always crosses a Schreier `H`-orbit. | State stabilizers can keep it in the same orbit. | Note 167 |
| SEM-1741 | Hash model boundary | Ideal hash ownership balances distinct states in expectation and gives remote-edge probability `1-1/P`. | Independent uniform model, not deterministic frontier guarantee. | Notes 51 and 167 |
| SEM-1742 | Matrix rule | Per-level source/destination matrices should separate occurrences, support arcs, accepted states, payload bytes, and protocol bytes. | Distributed BFS telemetry. | Notes 160, 165, and 167 |
| SEM-1743 | Authority boundary | Generator locality removes routing for an occurrence, not visited, convergence, publication, or completion obligations. | Exact distributed BFS. | Notes 52 and 167 |
| SEM-1744 | Proved quotient metric | `d_(G/H)(H,gH)=min_(x in gH)d_G(e,x)`. | Right Cayley action, normal `H`, and projected generator set. | Note 168 |
| SEM-1745 | Fiber boundary | Quotient distance is exact to the nearest fiber member, not every member. | Same quotient contract. | Notes 17 and 168 |
| SEM-1746 | Owner activation | Normal-quotient distance equals the first depth at which its coset can contain an original frontier state. | One block per coset and source identity. | Note 168 |
| SEM-1747 | Rejected implication | Quotient frontier width equals the number of owners carrying the original frontier at that depth. | Activated fibers retain later internal states. | Note 168 |
| SEM-1748 | Lower-bound fact | Existential block-graph distance is no greater than concrete distance to the target block. | Every concrete edge projects to a block edge. | Notes 124 and 168 |
| SEM-1749 | Counterexample | Blocks `{s}`, `{x,y}`, `{t}` with edges `s->x` and `y->t` admit a spurious two-step block path. | Existential block graph without lifting. | Note 168 |
| SEM-1750 | Rejected implication | Every block path lifts to a concrete path. | Consecutive block arcs may use incompatible representatives. | Notes 17, 128, and 168 |
| SEM-1751 | Sufficient condition | Label-stable transition congruence makes deterministic quotient words lift step by step. | `x~y` implies `succ_s(x)~succ_s(y)` for each label. | Notes 17, 128, and 168 |
| SEM-1752 | Nonnormal boundary | Nonnormal left-coset ownership preserves subgroup-label locality but not representative-independent outside-label transitions. | Right Cayley action with blocks `gH`. | Notes 167 and 168 |
| SEM-1753 | Schreier boundary | Algebraic `H`-orbit blocks need a separate congruence proof for exact outside-label quotient BFS. | Right action on `K\G`. | Notes 167 and 168 |
| SEM-1754 | Prediction boundary | First block activation does not determine later frontier occupancy, routing, bytes, or critical time. | Distributed BFS. | Note 168 |
| SEM-1755 | Replay boundary | An abstract block path is not an executable connector without compatible representative/lifting evidence. | Quotient or owner graph. | Notes 17 and 168 |
| SEM-1756 | Reporting rule | Quotient distances must be labeled exact-fiber, lower-bound, or heuristic according to their proof. | Algebraic/abstract BFS. | Notes 163 and 168 |
| SEM-1757 | Definition | A fiber has a concrete distance multiset whose minimum is quotient distance under the normal Cayley contract. | Source identity and left-coset blocks `gH`; normality makes `gH=Hg`. | Notes 168 and 169 |
| SEM-1758 | Counterexample | In `Z_6` with `H={0,3}` and generators `+/-1`, owner fiber `H` is active at depths zero and three. | Exact undirected Cayley BFS. | Note 169 |
| SEM-1759 | Rejected implication | First quotient arrival settles or exhausts the entire fiber. | Fiber members can occur at later original depths. | Note 169 |
| SEM-1760 | Generation boundary | Subgroup-local labels `S intersect H` need not generate `H`. | Cayley ownership. | Notes 167 and 169 |
| SEM-1761 | Counterexample | In directed `Z_20`, local generator `+2` reaches `6` in three steps while outside labels `+1,+5` reach it in two. | Even-residue subgroup `H`. | Note 169 |
| SEM-1762 | Rejected implication | Generation of `H` by local labels makes fibers geodesically closed under the full metric. | Outside generators can leave and re-enter more cheaply. | Note 169 |
| SEM-1763 | Rejected formula | Concrete distance is universally quotient distance plus an independently minimized local-fiber distance. | Entry representatives and quotient revisits matter. | Notes 17, 168, and 169 |
| SEM-1764 | Authority boundary | Owner blocks shard state authority; they are not state-level visited objects or one-time search phases. | Exact distributed BFS. | Notes 51, 52, and 169 |
| SEM-1765 | DAG boundary | One quotient parent per block cannot reconstruct concrete shortest predecessors or multiplicities. | Shortest-path DAG output. | Notes 11 and 169 |
| SEM-1766 | Bidirectional boundary | Forward and reverse records in the same owner block are not necessarily a state intersection. | Exact bidirectional BFS. | Notes 08, 17, and 169 |
| SEM-1767 | Exactness boundary | Hierarchical quotient/fiber solving needs additional lifting, convexity, product-metric, boundary-DP, or covering structure. | Problem-specific proof. | Note 169 |
| SEM-1768 | Reporting rule | First activation, all active depths, re-entry arcs, retained state, bytes, and time are separate per-owner measurements. | Algebraic multi-GPU BFS. | Notes 165 and 169 |
| SEM-1769 | Definition | `mu(q)` counts generator labels with quotient image `q=sH`. | Normal Cayley quotient. | Note 170 |
| SEM-1770 | Proved conservation | `sum_q mu(q)=|S|` and `mu(H)=|S intersect H|`. | Labeled generator manifest. | Notes 167 and 170 |
| SEM-1771 | Proved alias condition | Two labels share a quotient owner image exactly when `t^-1s in H`. | Left-coset blocks `gH` and normal `H`. | Note 170 |
| SEM-1772 | Concrete-identity boundary | Equal quotient images imply equal owner blocks, not equal concrete Cayley endpoints. | Distinct group elements remain distinct under right multiplication. | Note 170 |
| SEM-1773 | Proved routing formula | `M_d(C,D)=f_d(C)mu(C^-1D)`. | One block per quotient coset and exact frontier histogram. | Note 170 |
| SEM-1774 | Proved row conservation | `sum_D M_d(C,D)=|S|f_d(C)`. | Same logical occurrence contract. | Note 170 |
| SEM-1775 | Proved global conservation | `sum_(C,D)M_d(C,D)=|S||F_d|`. | Complete frontier expansion. | Notes 160 and 170 |
| SEM-1776 | Counterexample | In `Z_8/H` with `H={0,4}`, labels `+1,+5` share an owner image but reach concrete states `1,5`. | Parent zero. | Note 170 |
| SEM-1777 | Coalescing formula | A coset-to-GPU map aggregates the quotient occurrence matrix while preserving its total. | Declared physical block map. | Note 170 |
| SEM-1778 | Evidence boundary | Logical occurrence routing is not physical record, message, retry, or wire-byte traffic. | Concrete protocol required. | Notes 165 and 170 |
| SEM-1779 | Convolution boundary | Normal Cayley structure makes owner occurrence traffic translation-invariant but does not determine visited or accepted states. | Quotient group action. | Note 170 |
| SEM-1780 | Direction fact | Reverse quotient-image multiplicity satisfies `mu_rev(q)=mu(q^-1)` for an exactly inverted manifest. | Transpose traversal. | Notes 75, 159, and 170 |
| SEM-1781 | Schreier boundary | Orbit-block routing cannot generally be predicted from one global quotient-image histogram. | Representative-dependent outside-label transitions. | Notes 167, 168, and 170 |
| SEM-1782 | Reporting rule | Predicted raw matrices, concrete endpoint bins, accepted states, physical records, messages, retries, and bytes are separate claims. | Distributed Cayley BFS. | Notes 163, 165, and 170 |
| SEM-1783 | Proved raw convolution | `y_(d+1)(D)=sum_C f_d(C)mu(C^-1D)`. | Normal Cayley quotient and complete raw expansion. | Notes 170 and 171 |
| SEM-1784 | Proved block bound | `0<=f_(d+1)(D)<=min(y_(d+1)(D),|D minus B_d|)`. | Exact concrete identity and visited. | Note 171 |
| SEM-1785 | Counterexample | Source sets `{0,1}` and `{0,5}` in directed `Z_8` have equal quotient histograms and raw destination counts but next-frontier sizes two and four. | `H={0,4}`, `S={+1,+2}`. | Note 171 |
| SEM-1786 | Rejected implication | Equal frontier block histograms determine equal next frontiers. | Within-block visited and convergence information is lost. | Note 171 |
| SEM-1787 | Semantic boundary | Quotient count transport is linear before concrete deduplication and visited subtraction. | Exact Cayley BFS. | Notes 25 and 171 |
| SEM-1788 | Equality condition | Accepted block count equals raw occurrence count exactly when endpoints are pairwise distinct and all unvisited. | Declared block and level. | Note 171 |
| SEM-1789 | Rejected implication | Cayley regularity prevents cross-parent endpoint convergence. | Group relations can merge different words. | Notes 35, 93, and 171 |
| SEM-1790 | Capacity bound | `f_(d+1)(D)<=|H|-|B_d intersect D|` for a Cayley left-coset block `gH`. | Finite subgroup and state-level visited. | Note 171 |
| SEM-1791 | Evidence boundary | Occurrence and capacity ceilings are not exact frontier predictions. | Adjacency and collision structure remain unknown. | Note 171 |
| SEM-1792 | Authority boundary | Owner-bin aggregation cannot replace concrete identity and authoritative visited. | Exact distributed BFS. | Notes 51, 52, and 171 |
| SEM-1793 | Modeling boundary | Block-count-only models cannot be universally exact for concrete frontier evolution. | Counterexample with identical coarse inputs. | Note 171 |
| SEM-1794 | Reporting rule | Raw occurrences, distinct endpoints, visited endpoints, accepted states, physical records, and bytes are separate per-block quantities. | One- and multi-GPU BFS. | Notes 160, 165, and 171 |
| SEM-1795 | Validation boundary | Histogram parity is weaker than exact frontier-set parity. | Collision-resolving canonical comparison required. | Notes 163 and 171 |
| SEM-1796 | Proved partition independence | `F_(d+1)=(union_i Post(P_i)) minus B_d` for any partition of exact `F_d`. | Complete successors, exact identity, and fixed completed ball. | Note 172 |
| SEM-1797 | Algebraic fact | Candidate membership union is associative, commutative, and idempotent. | Exact state sets. | Notes 25 and 172 |
| SEM-1798 | Qualified delivery fact | At-least-once candidate delivery can preserve distance-only membership without physical exactly-once execution. | No loss, exact merge, durable pending work, explicit capacity, and complete termination. | Notes 30 and 172 |
| SEM-1799 | Failure boundary | Idempotent visited insertion does not prevent an orphaned claim when frontier publication is lost. | Claim/publication are separate effects. | Notes 30 and 172 |
| SEM-1800 | Determinism boundary | Partition-independent frontier sets do not imply deterministic parents, frontier order, retries, or work. | Same-layer exact BFS. | Note 172 |
| SEM-1801 | Output algebra | Canonical parent can use total-order minimum as an associative, commutative, idempotent reduction. | All equal-depth contenders close before finalization. | Notes 19, 30, and 172 |
| SEM-1802 | Non-idempotence counterexample | Replaying one diamond predecessor contribution changes path count from two to three while distances remain correct. | Naive additive counting. | Notes 11, 30, and 172 |
| SEM-1803 | Contribution boundary | Path counts and labeled multiplicities require stable logical identities or equivalent clean recomputation. | Retry-capable execution. | Notes 30, 121, and 172 |
| SEM-1804 | Rejected implication | Canonical parent may finalize on the first arrival. | A later equal-depth contender can have a smaller canonical key. | Notes 19 and 172 |
| SEM-1805 | Cross-level boundary | Idempotent reached-set union does not make arbitrary-order irrevocable distance claims correct. | Deeper proposals can precede shallower ones. | Notes 18, 164, and 172 |
| SEM-1806 | Multi-GPU rule | Logical occurrences, unique membership, durable pending work, metadata contributions, and physical retries are separate protocol objects. | Distributed exact BFS. | Notes 30, 165, and 172 |
| SEM-1807 | Validation boundary | Final visited-set parity cannot validate deterministic parents or shortest-path counts. | Rich output contract. | Notes 37, 163, and 172 |
| SEM-1808 | Reporting rule | Each BFS output needs a declared merge algebra, completeness boundary, retry semantics, and finalization event. | Parallel/recoverable BFS. | Note 172 |
| SEM-1809 | Taxonomy | Exact BFS separates graph soundness/completeness, identity, metric, schedule, coverage, publication, output merge, termination safety/liveness, and evidence validity. | Fixed graph/search epoch. | Note 173 |
| SEM-1810 | Independence fact | Successor soundness does not imply successor completeness, and completeness does not imply soundness. | Omitted-real versus added-spurious edge fixtures. | Notes 55 and 173 |
| SEM-1811 | Independence fact | Correct successor enumeration does not imply exact visited identity, nor conversely. | Aliased states versus missing edge. | Notes 28, 55, and 173 |
| SEM-1812 | Metric boundary | Exact reachable membership does not imply exact requested distance metric. | Physical/logical step mismatch. | Notes 12, 34, and 173 |
| SEM-1813 | Schedule boundary | Exact graph and identity do not imply shortest labels under arbitrary irrevocable first claim. | Long branch claims before shorter branch. | Notes 18, 164, and 173 |
| SEM-1814 | Coverage boundary | Exact successors for every executed call do not prove that every required reached state is expanded. | Lost shard, record, or route. | Notes 30, 55, and 173 |
| SEM-1815 | Publication boundary | Reached membership does not imply durable pending-or-expanded lifecycle. | Orphaned visited state. | Notes 30, 172, and 173 |
| SEM-1816 | Output boundary | Exact scalar distances do not imply correct parents, labels, DAG, or path counts. | Diamond omission/retry. | Notes 11, 57, and 173 |
| SEM-1817 | Termination decomposition | Safety against early completion and liveness of eventual completion detection are independent. | Never-announce versus local-empty-with-message fixtures. | Notes 09, 18, and 173 |
| SEM-1818 | Evidence boundary | Runtime truth and adequacy/independence of its evidence are separate propositions. | Shared oracle bugs and weak aggregate checks. | Notes 55, 163, and 173 |
| SEM-1819 | Formalization rule | Theorems should consume only assumptions required for their declared output strength. | Witness, target, bounded, exhaustive, and rich-output claims differ. | Note 173 |
| SEM-1820 | Distributed transfer rule | One-GPU predicates transfer only after their quantified work, authority, metadata, termination, and evidence domains include every worker and channel. | Exact multi-GPU BFS. | Note 173 |
| SEM-1821 | Failure record | Expert independence-map consultation produced no content because the tool returned `fetch failed`. | One call; no recommendation used. | Note 173 and research log |
| SEM-1822 | Reporting rule | Successor completeness, traversal coverage, durable publication, output closure, and global completion must not share one unlabeled “complete” status. | BFS reports and proofs. | Note 173 |
| SEM-1823 | Definition | A strict-level root obligation is one logical `(frontier parent,label occurrence)` duty in the fixed graph epoch. | Complete exact frontier. | Note 174 |
| SEM-1824 | Conservation rule | Every logical obligation occupies exactly one semantic lifecycle state at a consistent cut. | Stable obligation identity and no silent loss. | Note 174 |
| SEM-1825 | Retirement boundary | Send, receive, claim, or acknowledgement alone need not retire an obligation before endpoint/output/publication effects complete. | Declared causal protocol. | Notes 30 and 174 |
| SEM-1826 | Retry boundary | Physical retries preserve one logical obligation identity rather than creating graph work. | At-least-once/recovery execution. | Notes 30, 172, and 174 |
| SEM-1827 | Counterexample | Sender decrement before receiver increment creates a transient false-zero gap for an in-flight message. | Naive distributed counter. | Note 174 |
| SEM-1828 | Safety-liveness rule | Lost credit threatens premature completion; leaked credit prevents eventual completion. | Termination accounting. | Notes 173 and 174 |
| SEM-1829 | GPU boundary | Running kernels, device buffers, asynchronous copies, and collectives can hold causal work absent from host queues. | GPU/distributed execution. | Notes 07 and 174 |
| SEM-1830 | Completed-level condition | Every root and child obligation must retire and every accepted state must be published without hidden failure. | Strict exact level. | Notes 162 and 174 |
| SEM-1831 | Target boundary | Total outstanding count, minimum unfinished key, and output-specific unfinished metadata are distinct summaries. | Early target stopping. | Notes 56, 57, and 174 |
| SEM-1832 | Dynamic-work rule | Label-correcting decreases create/reactivate causal obligations that must enter termination accounting before parent responsibility disappears. | Fair asynchronous relaxation. | Notes 18, 164, and 174 |
| SEM-1833 | Recovery boundary | Checkpointing must capture every obligation/channel state or recreate them from a completed ancestor cut. | Exact restart. | Notes 30 and 174 |
| SEM-1834 | Epoch boundary | Credits and acknowledgements from another graph/search/ownership epoch cannot retire current work. | Repartition or restart. | Notes 30, 52, and 174 |
| SEM-1835 | Evidence boundary | Per-rank counters from inconsistent times do not prove a real conserved global cut. | Distributed telemetry. | Notes 163, 173, and 174 |
| SEM-1836 | Reporting rule | Logical obligations, lifecycle locations, child publication work, physical retries, unfinished keys, and cut IDs are separate fields. | Multi-GPU BFS termination. | Note 174 |
| SEM-1837 | Counterexample | Minimum parent state ID selects word `ba` while shortlex requires `ab`. | Labels `a<b`, parent IDs `p<q`. | Notes 19 and 175 |
| SEM-1838 | Definition | Candidate shortlex key is `(canonical parent-word rank, label rank)` at one exact depth. | Deterministic labeled transitions. | Note 175 |
| SEM-1839 | Proved recurrence | Minimum candidate key selects the lexicographically least length-`d+1` word for each child. | Complete equal-depth proposals and correct depth-`d` ranks. | Note 175 |
| SEM-1840 | Proved ranking step | Ordering selected minimum keys gives canonical word ranks for the next frontier. | Distinct deterministic words identify at most one state. | Note 175 |
| SEM-1841 | Identity boundary | Canonical word rank is path-order metadata, not semantic state identity or visited key. | Fixed output epoch. | Notes 28 and 175 |
| SEM-1842 | Reduction boundary | Per-child minimum and global next-frontier word ranking are separate closures. | Distributed shortlex BFS. | Note 175 |
| SEM-1843 | Distributed rule | Local pre-dedup must retain the minimum contender and owners must close all equal-depth proposals. | Partition-independent canonical output. | Notes 172 and 175 |
| SEM-1844 | Rejected implication | Concatenated owner-local state-ID ranks form global shortlex order. | State and path orders can disagree. | Note 175 |
| SEM-1845 | Parity boundary | Exact frontier-set parity across GPU counts does not validate canonical parent/word parity. | Rich deterministic output. | Notes 163 and 175 |
| SEM-1846 | Epoch boundary | Generator alphabet/order, action side, source, path identity, and quotient target define the canonical-word epoch. | Shortlex output. | Notes 16, 19, and 175 |
| SEM-1847 | Target boundary | First exact-depth target discovery proves scalar distance before it proves shortlex word closure. | Equal-depth smaller contenders may remain. | Notes 57, 162, and 175 |
| SEM-1848 | Quotient boundary | Quotient shortlex selects a least fiber word, not automatically a fixed-representative word. | Path lifting/target frame unproved. | Notes 17 and 175 |
| SEM-1849 | Reporting rule | Contender keys, first/canonical winner differences, ranks, epochs, closure, and canonical replay are distinct evidence fields. | Parallel deterministic BFS. | Note 175 |
| SEM-1850 | Reverse recurrence | Forward-oriented suffix candidate key is `(label rank,suffix rank)` because reverse BFS prepends the forward label. | Exact predecessor traversal. | Notes 40 and 176 |
| SEM-1851 | Counterexample | Reverse traversal order can select suffix `ba` while forward shortlex requires `ab`. | Labels `a<b` and two length-two suffixes. | Note 176 |
| SEM-1852 | Rejected implication | Reversing the inverse alphabet universally preserves forward suffix lexicographic order. | String reversal changes the comparison position. | Note 176 |
| SEM-1853 | Vertex-connector rule | For one fixed meeting state, canonical prefix and forward-oriented suffix give its least shortest connector word. | Both side reductions closed. | Note 176 |
| SEM-1854 | Edge-connector rule | A crossing edge contributes `prefix(u) + label + suffix(v)` when depths sum to the optimum. | Replay-valid directed edge. | Notes 08 and 176 |
| SEM-1855 | Split-depth boundary | Frontier-local prefix/suffix ranks are not directly comparable across connectors with different split depths. | Full concatenated lexical order required. | Note 176 |
| SEM-1856 | Completion decomposition | Shorter-path exclusion and equal-length lexical exclusion are separate completion predicates. | Canonical bidirectional output. | Notes 57 and 176 |
| SEM-1857 | Rejected implication | Distance-optimal first intersection is automatically shortlex-optimal. | Later equal-length connector can have a smaller word. | Notes 08 and 176 |
| SEM-1858 | Determinism boundary | Minimum meeting state/owner ID is not a canonical path-word rule. | Static state order need not match concatenated word order. | Notes 19 and 176 |
| SEM-1859 | Multi-GPU rule | Canonical bidirectional output needs closed prefix/suffix reductions and a global reduction over all optimal connectors. | Exact common identity and stable connector records. | Note 176 |
| SEM-1860 | Action boundary | Reverse operation uses inverse transformation while stored suffix and lexical order use forward labels. | Bijective Cayley moves. | Notes 40, 75, and 176 |
| SEM-1861 | Output boundary | One minimum connector and all-shortest connector/count outputs use different merge algebras. | `min` versus set/addition. | Notes 11, 172, and 176 |
| SEM-1862 | Reporting rule | Best distance, best canonical word, side ranks, connector identities, lexical closure, replay, and multiplicity are separate evidence fields. | Bidirectional BFS. | Note 176 |
| SEM-1863 | State-machine definition | Novelty claim, frontier publication, and expansion retirement are distinct semantic events. | Fixed graph/search epoch and exact state identity. | Notes 173, 174, and 178 |
| SEM-1864 | Publication invariant | Every visited state must be published/expanded or retain a live recoverable publication obligation. | Exact traversal coverage, not membership alone. | Note 178 |
| SEM-1865 | Counterexample | A worker can mark `b` visited on `s->a->b->c`, stop before enqueue, and make retry suppress the only expansion duty for `b`. | Blind already-visited drop after an orphaned claim. | Notes 173 and 178 |
| SEM-1866 | Rejected implication | Linearizable novelty insertion does not imply eventual frontier publication. | Claim and publication are separate effects. | Notes 173 and 178 |
| SEM-1867 | Sufficient proof pattern | Atomic joint commit is sufficient when it covers visited membership and a recoverable frontier duty as one semantic transaction. | Failure model and durability domain declared. | Note 178 |
| SEM-1868 | Sufficient proof pattern | A stable helpable claim descriptor can preserve responsibility when any observer either proves or completes idempotent publication. | Exact epoch-scoped descriptor, safe reclamation, explicit capacity failure. | Note 178 |
| SEM-1869 | Sufficient proof pattern | Log-before-claim can preserve replayable publication intent if log completeness, identity, epoch, and replay are exact. | Durable/equivalent intent and idempotent recovery. | Notes 30 and 178 |
| SEM-1870 | Conservation rule | A separately conserved publication obligation must be created before the candidate obligation retires. | Termination accounts for every causal child duty. | Notes 174 and 178 |
| SEM-1871 | Liveness boundary | Detecting an outstanding publication credit does not by itself guarantee that some agent can finish or recover it. | Failure may remove the original winner. | Note 178 |
| SEM-1872 | Visibility rule | Payload publication must precede visible `PUBLISHED` status, and observing that status must precede payload consumption. | Concrete host/device/network ordering mechanism remains runtime-specific. | Note 178 |
| SEM-1873 | Schedule boundary | Physical visibility of a depth-tagged frontier record does not authorize its early expansion under a closed-level contract. | Correct depth/epoch and schedule finalization. | Notes 164 and 178 |
| SEM-1874 | Output boundary | Duplicate publication may be harmless for set union while corrupting counts, all-parent data, or first-winner canonical output. | Merge algebra and contribution identity declared. | Notes 11, 172, and 178 |
| SEM-1875 | Evidence boundary | Note 178 supplies a conceptual publication contract, and REF-046 later supplies a bounded sequential interleaving model, not runtime or memory-model validation. | Six local and eighteen three-edge single-stop schedules only; recovery and idempotent publication are modeled assumptions. | Note 178; completed REF-046 report dated 2026-08-29 |
| SEM-1876 | Preserved failure | Early discovery-publication probe attempts did not run because Docker server access returned named-pipe permission denied. | Historical failed attempts only; REF-046 later completed without turning this failure record into a runtime protocol proof. | REF-046 not-run report and completed 2026-08-29 report |
| SEM-1877 | Readiness correction | Empty docker ps output without positive server identity was insufficient to establish Docker readiness at the recorded attempt. | Subsequent server queries failed then; this does not assert current Docker availability. | Historical REF-046 not-run report |
| SEM-1878 | Test-first history | Early attempts stopped before implementation because the Docker RED step could not execute; a later attempt completed the bounded model and corrected two test-harness issues. | TDD and Docker-only contract; later result remains a finite sequential model. | Both REF-046 reports |
| SEM-1879 | Communication taxonomy | Active cross-owner edges, information absent at a receiver, and physical protocol traffic are distinct BFS quantities. | Partition, initial knowledge, and output contract declared. | Note 179 |
| SEM-1880 | Structural definition | `X_d` is the set of active depth-`d` edges whose endpoints have different declared vertex owners. | Fixed frontier and ownership epoch. | Note 179 |
| SEM-1881 | Rejected implication | One active cut edge does not universally force one network message. | Adjacency/frontier facts may be replicated or destination-computable. | Note 179 star-layout counterexample |
| SEM-1882 | Lower-bound boundary | A nonzero BFS communication lower bound requires restrictions on initial placement, memory/replication, redundant work, output placement, uncertainty, or rounds. | Complete graph/source replication permits zero traversal communication at replicated work/storage cost. | Note 179 |
| SEM-1883 | Information bound | Learning an arbitrary `k`-subset of a known `N`-state universe with no useful prior knowledge needs at least `ceil(log2 binomial(N,k))` bits in the worst case. | Exact subset reconstruction and declared conditional knowledge. | Counting argument; note 179 |
| SEM-1884 | Counterexample | The same cross-owner star cut can require many child identities or one active-root fact depending on where adjacency is initially stored. | Same graph and vertex owners; different edge knowledge. | Note 179 |
| SEM-1885 | Causal-amplification boundary | One bridge message can activate an arbitrarily large later remote BFS wave. | Two large subgraphs joined by one bridge. | Note 179; related REF-043/044 observations |
| SEM-1886 | Reporting coordinates | Cross-owner occurrences, unique remote states, active owner pairs, encoded payload bits, actual bytes, and rounds must be reported separately. | Per-level declared protocol. | Notes 165 and 179 |
| SEM-1887 | Rejected implication | Equal remote fractions or unique remote-state counts do not imply equal communication cost. | Owner-pair fanout, state width, framing, and rounds may differ. | Note 179 |
| SEM-1888 | Dedup location rule | Producer filtering can reduce routed occurrences, whereas owner-only dedup cannot recover bytes already transmitted. | Exactness of any producer-side rejection proved separately. | Notes 52, 157, and 179 |
| SEM-1889 | Replication boundary | Replication can exchange candidate traffic for graph/frontier/visited synchronization, memory, redundant work, and epoch-management costs. | Preprocessing and steady-state costs kept separate. | Note 179 |
| SEM-1890 | Cayley boundary | Shared generator actions can make destination-side recomputation cheap without making sharded frontier/visited information common knowledge. | Exact action and identity; distributed frontier/visited. | Notes 06, 167, and 179 |
| SEM-1891 | Algebraic-routing boundary | Predictable coset/quotient ownership determines where information belongs but does not prove that no information must be transferred. | Destination lacks some active frontier/identity fact. | Notes 167--170 and 179 |
| SEM-1892 | Distribution interpretation | 1D versus 2D graph distributions rearrange initial adjacency knowledge and collective structure, not only the numerical vertex edge cut. | Distributed-memory BFS model. | Buluç--Madduri; note 179 |
| SEM-1893 | Compression boundary | Sieving/compression can reduce protocol bytes while leaving structural cut occurrences fixed. | Encoding and filter correctness declared. | Lu et al.; note 179 |
| SEM-1894 | Evidence boundary | Note 179 is a conceptual decomposition and does not establish a universal lower bound or real interconnect measurement. | Future bounded model and runtime evidence remain absent. | Note 179 |
| SEM-1895 | Validation definition | Exact distributed BFS set reconciliation asks whether the semantic symmetric difference is empty. | Canonical exact state identity and fixed validation epoch. | Notes 163 and 180 |
| SEM-1896 | Output boundary | Frontier-set equality does not validate distance maps, parents, labeled DAGs, path counts, multisets, or ordered artifacts. | Requested comparison object declared. | Notes 36, 57, 163, and 180 |
| SEM-1897 | Information lower bound | Deterministic equality of two arbitrary `N`-bit membership vectors requires linear communication in `N`. | Always-correct two-party protocol; no correlated-placement assumption. | Equality fooling/pigeonhole argument; note 180 |
| SEM-1898 | Digest boundary | A fixed small deterministic digest cannot prove arbitrary large-set equality unless it is injective on the declared family. | Unrestricted membership vectors otherwise. | Notes 28, 163, and 180 |
| SEM-1899 | Exact distributed method | Identically sharded injective-rank bitmaps can be compared wordwise locally and reduced by a global mismatch OR without central gathering. | Every corresponding word co-located and every verifier completes. | Note 180 |
| SEM-1900 | Interpretation boundary | A one-bit global mismatch reduction is exact only because the full data were already compared locally; it is not a one-bit digest of unseen inputs. | Complete exact local comparisons. | Note 180 |
| SEM-1901 | Exact distributed method | Canonical full-state sorted streams admit exact merge comparison for wide implicit states. | Injective serialization, complete streams, explicit multiplicity and failures. | Note 180 |
| SEM-1902 | Exact distributed method | Hash-based verifier routing remains exact when collisions only co-locate records and full keys/multiplicities are compared. | Complete normalization shuffle and collision resolution. | Notes 28, 51, and 180 |
| SEM-1903 | Verifier-epoch rule | Results with different worker counts or owner maps must normalize by semantic key into a common immutable verifier partition. | Validation map independent of runtime owner layout. | Note 180 |
| SEM-1904 | Completeness boundary | Exact local comparators do not prove global equality if a source shard or in-flight verifier record can be omitted. | Shuffle/publication/termination evidence required. | Notes 163, 174, 178, and 180 |
| SEM-1905 | Merkle boundary | Equal Merkle roots imply equal artifacts only under hash collision assumptions or proved finite injectivity plus canonical tree encoding. | Layout, length, padding, and domain separation fixed. | Merkle; note 180 |
| SEM-1906 | IBLT boundary | IBLT difference listing is high-probability reconciliation, not unconditional exact equality without exact validation or fallback. | Load/hash/failure model declared. | Goodrich--Mitzenmacher; note 180 |
| SEM-1907 | Witness rule | An exact symmetric-difference state is a replayable falsification witness even though one witness does not quantify total damage. | Exact key and provenance retained. | Note 180 |
| SEM-1908 | Independence boundary | Exact CPU/GPU or run/run set parity does not prove intended-graph correctness when both share an oracle/encoding defect. | Common-mode bug possible. | Notes 55, 163, 173, and 180 |
| SEM-1909 | GPU validation boundary | Device-local exact comparisons require completed kernels, visible payloads, capacity status, and a consistent validation cut before global reduction. | GPU/multi-GPU verifier. | Notes 163, 174, 178, and 180 |
| SEM-1910 | Rejected implication | Equal per-owner counts do not prove equality, especially across different owner maps. | Compensating membership errors and repartitioning. | Note 180 |
| SEM-1911 | Reporting rule | Normalization traffic, exact local comparison, digest evidence, mismatch witnesses, reduction, replay, and oracle independence are distinct validation claims. | Distributed BFS artifact validation. | Note 180 |
| SEM-1912 | Evidence boundary | Note 180 supplies a conceptual reconciliation contract but no retained bounded or runtime validation measurement of that reconciliation protocol. | Its separate gate remains deferred; completion of the different REF-046 publication model does not validate reconciliation. | Note 180 |
| SEM-1913 | Forgetting criterion | A closed-state record may be reclaimed only when no future admissible occurrence needs to distinguish it from unseen, or equivalent boundary information remains. | Graph, schedule, output, and failure model declared. | Note 181 |
| SEM-1914 | Undirected rolling theorem | While expanding exact `F_d` in an undirected unit graph, every neighbor lies in `F_(d-1)`, `F_d`, or `F_(d+1)`. | Complete strict levels and exact identity. | Triangle inequality; notes 41 and 181 |
| SEM-1915 | Storage corollary | Previous, current, and building-next exact layer sets suffice for scalar duplicate rejection in strict undirected BFS. | Publication closes before rotation; richer historical outputs excluded. | Note 181 |
| SEM-1916 | Correction | Undirected cycles do not invalidate the three-layer rolling theorem; the earlier note-45 wording was too broad. | Unit undirected graph. | Corrected note 45; note 181 |
| SEM-1917 | Output boundary | Safe reclamation for scalar novelty can still destroy final reached-set materialization, distance queries, parents, DAGs, counts, canonical words, and restart state. | Output-specific retention. | Notes 11, 30, 57, and 181 |
| SEM-1918 | Directed counterexample | Edge `c->s` on `s->a->b->c` can reintroduce depth-zero source after a short rolling window forgets it. | Directed strict BFS with no permanent old certificate. | Note 181 |
| SEM-1919 | DAG counterexample | A directed acyclic graph can have an edge from arbitrary BFS depth `k` back to a different vertex of depth one. | Two source branches and edge `a_k->x`; no return path. | Note 181 |
| SEM-1920 | Backward-span definition | `beta=sup({0} union {d(u)-d(v)})` in `N union {infinity}` measures reachable directed edge jumps toward earlier BFS layers. | Fixed source; supremum covers unbounded infinite graphs. | Corrected note 181 |
| SEM-1921 | Bounded-window theorem | If finite `beta<=L` is independently proved, retaining `F_(max(0,d-L))..F_d` plus building next suffices for scalar old-state rejection at depth `d`. | Strict completed layers and exact layer sets. | Corrected note 181 |
| SEM-1922 | Circularity boundary | Measuring exact backward span from completed BFS distances is evidence after traversal, not automatically a prior memory-design proof. | Structural bound otherwise needed. | Note 181 |
| SEM-1923 | Cayley inverse bound | For edge `x->xg`, an `S`-word for `g^-1` of length `L_g` implies `d(x)-d(xg)<=L_g`. | Right action and directed `S`-word metric. | Word concatenation proof; note 181 |
| SEM-1924 | Symmetric Cayley corollary | A symmetric generator set has backward radial span at most one. | Every generator inverse is in the alphabet. | Notes 16 and 181 |
| SEM-1925 | Boundary-certificate principle | Frontier search replaces forgotten Closed membership with exact used-transition information on a solid active boundary. | Required neighbors/predecessors and metadata are complete. | Korf et al.; note 181 |
| SEM-1926 | Reconstruction trade | Divide-and-conquer re-search can recover one optimal path after parents are discarded but does not reproduce every richer original artifact. | Suitable intermediate optimal states and exact subsearches. | Korf et al.; note 181 |
| SEM-1927 | Rejected implication | Delayed duplicate detection changes when exact membership is checked but does not by itself justify deleting old membership. | Exact closure still required before expansion. | Notes 15 and 181 |
| SEM-1928 | Multi-GPU reclamation rule | A layer/descriptor may be reused only after a consistent cut closes every kernel, message, retry, publication, checkpoint, and output dependency that can reference it. | Distributed/GPU execution. | Notes 174, 178, and 181 |
| SEM-1929 | Cost boundary | Rolling windows trade ball storage for boundary metadata, regeneration, merge work, reconstruction, stronger reclamation, or checkpoint complexity. | Work/storage/output costs reported separately. | Notes 15, 165, and 181 |
| SEM-1930 | Evidence boundary | Note 181 supplies conceptual proofs/counterexamples but no retained bounded or runtime memory measurement for visited reclamation. | Separate gate deferred; do not infer current Docker state from an earlier REF-046 failure. | Note 181 |
| SEM-1931 | Boundary taxonomy | Metric layer, physical Open/queue, processed-side live boundary, unprocessed-side live boundary, crossing edge cut, and owner boundary are distinct objects. | Fixed graph, root, order, and ownership when applicable. | Notes 48, 73, 179, and 182 |
| SEM-1932 | Left-boundary definition | `L_i` contains processed vertices having at least one neighbor in the unprocessed suffix. | Undirected graph and total order. | Note 182 |
| SEM-1933 | Right-boundary definition | `R_i` contains unprocessed vertices having at least one neighbor in the processed prefix. | Undirected graph and total order. | Note 182 |
| SEM-1934 | Pathwidth theorem | Minimum maximum processed-side vertex separation over all orders equals graph pathwidth under the standard minus-one convention. | Finite undirected graph. | Kinnersley 1992; note 182 |
| SEM-1935 | BFS-constrained width | Minimum vertex separation over orders nondecreasing in root distance is at least ordinary pathwidth. | Fixed root and exact distances. | Order-set inclusion; note 182 |
| SEM-1936 | Star counterexample | Center-rooted `K_(1,n-1)` has BFS layer/Open width `n-1` while processed-side separation of its BFS order is one. | Conventional materialized queue for the Open-width statement. | Note 182 |
| SEM-1937 | Rejected implication | Small processed-side vertex separation does not guarantee a small materialized BFS Open list. | Left and right/open boundaries may differ. | Star family; note 182 |
| SEM-1938 | Binary-tree counterexample | At the penultimate/last-layer cut of a height-`h` complete binary tree, every BFS order has left boundary `2^(h-1)` and right boundary `2^h`. | Natural root and nondecreasing-distance order. | Note 182 |
| SEM-1939 | Separation gap | Complete binary trees have pathwidth `O(h)` but exponential BFS-constrained boundary from the natural root. | Depth-oriented path decomposition upper bound. | Note 182 |
| SEM-1940 | Schedule boundary | A low-separation arbitrary order need not reveal vertices in nondecreasing source distance. | Another distance/relaxation/decomposition mechanism would be required. | Notes 164 and 182 |
| SEM-1941 | Edge/vertex boundary | Left/right live-vertex counts and crossing-edge count can differ by degree and endpoint concentration. | Fixed order cut. | Note 182 |
| SEM-1942 | Rolling-window boundary | Note 181's recent-layer window is sufficient for scalar undirected BFS but is not a universal minimum live-boundary representation. | Complete strict levels. | Notes 181 and 182 |
| SEM-1943 | Output-liveness boundary | Parent, DAG, count, and canonical metadata can remain live after graph-boundary duplicate obligations end. | Rich BFS outputs. | Notes 57, 175, 176, and 182 |
| SEM-1944 | Multi-GPU decomposition | Temporal processed/future boundaries and static local/remote ownership boundaries are independent cuts. | Distributed BFS. | Notes 51, 179, and 182 |
| SEM-1945 | Cayley boundary | Vertex transitivity does not imply small metric spheres or BFS-constrained live boundaries. | Fixed generator metric. | Notes 46, 93, 94, and 182 |
| SEM-1946 | Schreier boundary | Stabilizer state aliases can reduce distinct boundary states without proportionally reducing labeled occurrence metadata. | Fixed action and output identity. | Notes 158--160 and 182 |
| SEM-1947 | Representation boundary | Vertex-separation/pathwidth counts do not directly imply bytes because ranks, compression, regeneration, state width, and metadata vary. | Declared knowledge and representation family. | Notes 36, 179, and 182 |
| SEM-1948 | Evidence boundary | Note 182 gives exact conceptual families but no retained queue measurement or computed optimal pathwidth corpus. | Separate bounded gate deferred; REF-046 does not execute this experiment. | Note 182 |
| SEM-1949 | Success taxonomy | Witness discovery, pointwise distance convergence, and certified distance finality are distinct on countably branching graphs. | Effective exact successor presentation. | Note 183 |
| SEM-1950 | Presentation definition | A countable successor enumerator may eventually emit every occurrence without ever certifying completion. | Every emitted edge exact and finite-indexed. | Notes 09 and 183 |
| SEM-1951 | Strict-layer failure | Infinite root branching can prevent a strict level-closed BFS from expanding an early depth-one child toward a depth-two target. | Nonterminating complete root enumeration. | Notes 09 and 183 |
| SEM-1952 | Dovetail completeness | Fair enumeration of all finite successor-index tuples eventually finds every reachable target having a finite indexed path witness. | Countable effective branching and fair simulation. | Diagonal enumeration proof; note 183 |
| SEM-1953 | Semi-decision boundary | Dovetail witness completeness does not imply completed metric balls or unreachable-target termination. | Infinite layers may remain open forever. | Note 183 |
| SEM-1954 | First-hit counterexample | A length-two target path through early child `c_0` can be found before the same target appears as late root successor `E_s(M)`. | Fair non-layered enumeration. | Note 183 |
| SEM-1955 | Rejected implication | Fair countable enumeration does not preserve first-hit shortest-path ordering. | Shallower alternatives may have later enumeration indices. | Note 183 |
| SEM-1956 | Label-setting boundary | Irrevocable first visited claim can freeze a nonshortest distance under dovetail scheduling. | Later smaller proposal suppressed. | Notes 18, 164, and 183 |
| SEM-1957 | Pointwise convergence | Fair label-correcting relaxation eventually gives the exact label for every target with a fixed finite shortest witness. | Every causal edge/decrease reactivated fairly; exact unit edges. | Shortest-path induction; note 183 |
| SEM-1958 | Finality boundary | Eventual stabilization of a target label need not have a finitely detectable stabilization time. | Infinite shorter-path alternative space. | Note 183 |
| SEM-1959 | Indistinguishable-prefix argument | Any finite transcript without direct `s->t` is compatible with an extension where that edge appears at a later enumeration index. | Positive enumerator supplies no completion certificate. | Note 183 |
| SEM-1960 | Extra-oracle rule | Certified shortest finality under infinite branching needs finite smaller-depth closure or another exact negative/lower-bound certificate. | Incumbent path alone is an upper bound. | Notes 42, 48, 56, and 183 |
| SEM-1961 | Finite-state boundary | A finite semantic state set does not guarantee terminating work when its successor presentation emits infinite redundant occurrences without completion. | Exact identity but nonterminating enumeration. | Note 183 |
| SEM-1962 | Infinite-alphabet Cayley boundary | With countably infinite generators, an early multi-letter word may precede a late one-letter representation of the same element. | Exact equality and effective generator enumeration. | Note 183 |
| SEM-1963 | Finite-alphabet scope | Ordinary finite-move puzzle Cayley graphs are locally finite, so note 183's infinite-branching failure does not apply to that declared move graph. | Finite everywhere-applicable move alphabet. | Notes 06, 09, and 183 |
| SEM-1964 | GPU boundary | Finite GPU batches can advance prefixes of infinite enumerators but cannot make an infinite layer semantically closed. | Countably infinite branching. | Note 183 |
| SEM-1965 | Reporting rule | Enumerator fairness, witness depth, incumbent bounds, label decreases, unfinished indices, and certified finality are separate fields. | Dovetailed/distributed search. | Note 183 |
| SEM-1966 | Evidence boundary | Note 183's claims are conceptual infinite-family proofs; bounded finite experiments cannot establish universal enumerator completion behavior. | Docker irrelevant and not retried. | Note 183 |
| SEM-1967 | Source-audit fact | Pinned DeepCubeA Cube3 search states contain 54 unique sticker IDs; division by nine occurs only when deriving the six-class neural-network input. | Official `environments/cube3.py` at commit `919489f14ecbbc80dc1bf1539ac0a462ffaca7c5`; source inspection, not runtime execution. | Note 184 |
| SEM-1968 | Transfer criterion | CayleyPy-to-DeepCubeA labeled action equivalence requires one simultaneous 54-position bijection and one explicit signed-generator map satisfying every generator conjugacy equation. | Full permutation actions; separate per-face renamings are insufficient. | Note 184 |
| SEM-1969 | Output boundary | Unlabeled graph isomorphism can transfer distances and sphere sizes, but replayable move words and canonical labeled outputs require the generator-label map. | Exact graph actions; output contract declared. | Note 184 |
| SEM-1970 | Rejected inference | Equal generator orders and shallow sphere counts do not prove that two independently specified labeled Cube actions are conjugate. | Necessary fingerprints are not a simultaneous action witness. | REF-029 and note 184 |
| SEM-1971 | Identity boundary | DeepCubeA's six-class neural observation must not replace its 54-ID exact state equality in BFS visited checks. | The observation map is many-to-one. | Note 184 |
| SEM-1972 | Evidence status | REF-029 executed the CayleyPy fixture but did not execute the DeepCubeA oracle or a simultaneous coordinate conjugacy comparison. | Historical fixture scope; neither current Docker state nor simultaneous action equivalence follows from it. | REF-029 and note 184 |
| SEM-1973 | State-map correction | For unique-sticker arrays, a coordinate bijection must rename both positions and sticker IDs: `T_q(x)(q(i))=q(x(i))`; a one-sided coordinate reorder generally does not preserve solved state. | Sticker identities use the solved-position namespace. | Note 185; correction to note 184 |
| SEM-1974 | Tuple criterion | Labeled permutation actions are equivalent exactly when one position bijection and one label map satisfy the conjugacy equation for every generator simultaneously. | Common normalized action convention. | Notes 184-185 |
| SEM-1975 | Counterexample | Pairwise conjugacy of corresponding generators does not imply simultaneous tuple conjugacy. | The three-transposition `S_3` tuple counterexample maps two distinct source components to one target component. | Note 185 |
| SEM-1976 | Rejected transfer | Isomorphism of the abstract generated groups does not identify their labeled permutation actions. | Action and point stabilizers remain additional data. | Note 185 |
| SEM-1977 | Orbit-propagation fact | With fixed label map and anchors, equivariance forces the position map on the entire generated orbit. | Exact deterministic permutation actions. | Note 185 |
| SEM-1978 | Stabilizer criterion | A transitive anchored action map is well-defined when source anchor stabilizer words also stabilize the target anchor; under equal finite orbit and invertible translation this becomes stabilizer equality. | Declared group/label translation and anchor pair. | Note 185 |
| SEM-1979 | Nonuniqueness fact | Any two simultaneous conjugacies differ by a permutation centralizing every target generator, and every such composition is another conjugacy. | Fixed source tuple, target tuple, and label map. | Note 185 |
| SEM-1980 | Anchor fact | In a transitive action, a commuting action automorphism that fixes one anchor fixes the entire orbit. | Transitivity under the generator tuple. | Note 185 |
| SEM-1981 | Fingerprint boundary | Generator cycle types, inverse pairs, commutation matrices, short relations, and shallow sphere counts can reject candidates but do not replace the full simultaneous equations. | Selected finite fingerprints only. | Notes 184-185 |
| SEM-1982 | Canonical-output boundary | A labeled action conjugacy preserves translated shortest words, but it preserves shortlex canonicality only when the alphabet-order translation is also declared compatible. | Exact action conjugacy and declared lexical orders. | Note 185 |
| SEM-1983 | Performance boundary | Semantically conjugate position numberings can have different locality, hashes, owner routing, memory traffic, and elapsed time. | Same abstract labeled action, different physical encoding. | Note 185 |
| SEM-1984 | Preservation theorem | After edge/vertex deletions, an old finite source distance survives exactly for vertices reachable from the source in the surviving old complete shortest-path DAG. | Finite unit-edge graph; complete old predecessor DAG; declared failure identity. | Note 186 |
| SEM-1985 | Phase boundary | The old shortest DAG exactly classifies preserved versus invalid old labels but does not generally compute increased replacement distances. | Longer detours may use arcs outside the old DAG. | Notes 118 and 186 |
| SEM-1986 | Support recurrence | A nonfailed old-depth vertex preserves its label exactly when at least one surviving old shortest predecessor itself preserves its label. | Depth-ordered complete old DAG. | Note 186 |
| SEM-1987 | Tree boundary | For deletion of a selected BFS-tree edge, the scalar invalidation set is contained in, but can be strictly smaller than, the selected child subtree. | Complete alternative shortest paths may survive. | Diamond counterexample in note 186 |
| SEM-1988 | Non-tree distance fact | Deleting an edge outside a retained BFS tree preserves every scalar source distance because the complete selected tree witnesses survive. | One edge deletion; fixed source; surviving vertices. | Note 186 |
| SEM-1989 | Output boundary | Deleting a non-tree edge can still change the shortest-predecessor DAG, shortest-path counts, or canonical outputs. | Deleted edge participates in the relevant richer contract. | Note 186 |
| SEM-1990 | Dominance characterization | Under one old-DAG edge failure, old-label invalidation is exactly edge dominance in the old shortest-path DAG. | Single edge; source-shortest DAG, not full graph. | Notes 89 and 186 |
| SEM-1991 | Batch-failure boundary | Several failures can jointly hit every old shortest path even when no single failure dominates the target. | Combined deletion set; exact `D-F` reachability required. | Note 186 |
| SEM-1992 | Count-cone fact | Deleting one old-DAG arc removes at least one old shortest path from every old-DAG descendant of its head; preserved-distance descendants strictly lose path count under edge-occurrence identity. | Complete old DAG and exact nonoverflow counts. | Note 186 |
| SEM-1993 | Cayley deletion transfer | A global generator-label deletion preserves an old distance exactly when some old shortest word avoids every removed-label occurrence. | Declared labeled Cayley/Schreier action and failure family. | Notes 118 and 186 |
| SEM-1994 | Parallel reporting rule | Decremental execution must separate old-label preservation, invalidation, finite repair, new unreachability, and richer-output changes. | Parallel/distributed dynamic BFS. | Note 186 |
| SEM-1995 | Single-insertion formula | After inserting directed unit arc `(a,b)`, every new source distance is `min(d_G(s,v), d_G(s,a)+1+d_G(b,v))`. | Finite directed unit-edge graph; one semantic arc insertion. | Note 187 |
| SEM-1996 | Decrease-cone criterion | Some scalar distance decreases iff the inserted head improves: `d_G(s,a)+1<d_G(s,b)`. | Same single directed insertion contract. | Triangle-inequality proof in note 187 |
| SEM-1997 | Equality-cone fact | If the inserted arc gives an equal-length new route to `b`, unchanged-distance richer outputs change exactly for targets whose old shortest route can pass through `b`. | Complete old distances; semantic edge identity. | Note 187 |
| SEM-1998 | Irrelevance fact | If `d_G(s,a)+1>d_G(s,b)`, the inserted arc belongs to no shortest source-target path. | Unit positive edges and old exact distances. | Note 187 |
| SEM-1999 | Count formula | For a target where the inserted-edge candidate is shortest, the number of new through-edge shortest paths is `sigma_s(a)*sigma_b(v)`. | Exact finite nonoverflow counts and declared path identity. | Note 187 |
| SEM-2000 | DAG boundary | The insertion suffix is shortest from the new edge head and need not lie in the old source-rooted predecessor DAG. | Directed unit graph and strict/equal insertion cone. | Note 187 |
| SEM-2001 | Undirected formula | One inserted undirected edge requires the minimum of the old distance and both oriented through-edge candidates. | Finite undirected unit graph; edge used at most once by a shortest path. | Note 187 |
| SEM-2002 | Batch counterexample | Independent one-edge sensitivities do not solve multiple insertions because a new shortest path can chain several new arcs. | Three isolated vertices plus inserted path `s->x->t`. | Note 187 |
| SEM-2003 | Cayley update boundary | Adding one generator is a translated edge-family insertion, and new shortest words may use that generator repeatedly. | Declared Cayley/Schreier action. | Notes 22 and 187 |
| SEM-2004 | Rejected propagation | Not every graph descendant of an improved inserted head decreases distance. | Exact inequality with old head-rooted distance selects the cone. | Note 187 |
| SEM-2005 | Output boundary | An insertion can leave every scalar distance unchanged while adding predecessor-DAG arcs, shortest paths, or changing canonical output. | Equal-length insertion cone. | Note 187 |
| SEM-2006 | Parallel reporting rule | Incremental execution should separate strict label decreases, equal-label contributions, proposals, duplicates, and accepted changes. | Parallel/distributed dynamic BFS. | Note 187 |
| SEM-2007 | Batch path decomposition | Every path after a finite insertion batch uniquely alternates old-graph subpaths with its ordered sequence of inserted-edge occurrences. | Finite directed positive unit-edge graph; semantic edge identity declared. | Note 188 |
| SEM-2008 | Bounded-use recurrence | `D_(r+1)(v)=min(D_r(v), min_(a,b)[D_r(a)+1+d_G(b,v)])` gives shortest paths using at most `r+1` inserted arcs. | Exact old pair distances and min-plus infinity arithmetic. | Note 188 |
| SEM-2009 | Finite closure fact | After at most `|F|` inserted-edge-use rounds, the recurrence equals exact updated distance. | A positive-cost shortest simple path repeats no concrete inserted edge. | Note 188 |
