| SEM-2010 | Endpoint metric equivalence | A weighted terminal graph containing exact old terminal distances and inserted unit arcs preserves updated scalar distances after an old-metric suffix. | Complete finite terminal set of source and update endpoints. | Note 188 |
| SEM-2011 | Cost boundary | Exact endpoint compression does not imply it is cheaper than fresh BFS because required old terminal-distance rows are part of the workload. | Representation and reuse regime declared. | Note 188 |
| SEM-2012 | Round boundary | Inserted-edge-use closure rounds are not BFS depth; one old metric segment can contain many original hops. | Batch recurrence. | Note 188 |
| SEM-2013 | Counting counterexample | Naive path counting on the endpoint metric graph can count one old path several times through optional intermediate terminal subdivisions. | Additive path identity; redundant metric edges. | Note 188 |
| SEM-2014 | Algebra boundary | Redundant endpoint decompositions are harmless for idempotent minimum distance but not for non-idempotent path-count addition. | Exact min-plus versus additive merge contracts. | Notes 172 and 188 |
| SEM-2015 | Finalization boundary | Scalar batch-label convergence does not finalize equal-label predecessor, count, or canonical-output contributions. | Rich output contract and complete batch epoch. | Notes 57 and 188 |
| SEM-2016 | Version boundary | Publishing an intermediate at-most-`r` closure as an atomic batch result can miss paths using more inserted arcs. | Atomic named graph version `G+F`. | Notes 22 and 188 |
| SEM-2017 | Undirected identity boundary | Two oriented records for one inserted undirected edge are not two independent semantic edges for richer path outputs. | Declared undirected path identity. | Note 188 |
| SEM-2018 | Cayley family boundary | The `|F|` bound counts concrete inserted edge occurrences, not generator labels; one new label may repeat along a shortest word at distinct states. | Finite Cayley/Schreier action batch. | Note 188 |
| SEM-2019 | Distributed closure rule | Owner-local label stability cannot certify atomic batch completion while remote lower/equal proposals with additional inserted-edge uses remain possible. | Distributed exact closure and named batch epoch. | Note 188 |
| SEM-2020 | 1D phase contract | Vertex-source 1D BFS expands owner-local outgoing adjacency, routes candidates to destination owners, and performs authoritative visited/update before global level closure. | Explicit distributed unit graph and declared owner map. | Buluç-Madduri; note 189 |
| SEM-2021 | 2D phase contract | Checkerboard BFS uses frontier expand along one processor-grid dimension and candidate fold along the orthogonal dimension. | Declared matrix/vector orientation and processor-grid layout. | Buluç-Madduri; note 189 |
| SEM-2022 | Expand obligation | Every frontier vertex must reach every block holding one of its required outgoing edges. | Exact successor completeness; 2D explicit adjacency. | Note 189 |
| SEM-2023 | Fold obligation | Every generated destination must reach an authority with complete visited state for that destination and epoch. | Exact identity and authoritative ownership. | Note 189 |
| SEM-2024 | Termination boundary | Empty local expand/fold buffers do not prove level closure while source pieces, edge visits, candidates, or publications remain in flight. | Distributed level-synchronous BFS. | Notes 56, 174, and 189 |
| SEM-2025 | Communication decomposition | 2D partitioning trades frontier replication and two slice collectives against a process-wide candidate exchange; it does not eliminate communication. | Explicit checkerboard algorithm. | Buluç-Madduri; note 189 |
| SEM-2026 | Cut boundary | Static edge cut does not determine fold traffic because root, level, block aggregation, visited suppression, and metadata affect space-time candidate grouping. | Fixed edge partition; execution policy declared. | Buluç-Madduri partitioning study; notes 179 and 189 |
| SEM-2027 | Collective boundary | Fewer participants or bytes do not alone determine collective duration; topology, startup, congestion, algorithm, and overlap remain separate. | Named communicator and machine. | Notes 165, 179, and 189 |
| SEM-2028 | Output boundary | A 2D BFS tree implementation is not evidence for complete predecessor-DAG, path-count, or canonical output support. | Graph500-style one-tree versus richer contracts. | Graph500 specification; notes 57 and 189 |
| SEM-2029 | Memory boundary | 2D decomposition separates adjacency, frontier, visited metadata, and candidate-buffer placement, so total graph bytes divided by process count is not a peak-capacity proof. | Declared replication and lifetime. | Note 189 |
| SEM-2030 | Implicit-transfer boundary | An implicit graph has no stored adjacency nonzeros to checkerboard; a second partition axis must be introduced and proved complete. | Successor-oracle graph. | Notes 44 and 189 |
| SEM-2031 | Cayley shard obligation | Under generator sharding, every frontier state must reach every required generator shard and every full endpoint must reach its exact visited owner. | Complete declared generator alphabet and exact state identity. | Note 189 |
| SEM-2032 | Alias boundary | Cross-shard same-parent aliases may be discardable for state-set output but required for labeled DAG or path-count output. | Schreier/duplicate transformations and declared merge algebra. | Notes 157, 172, and 189 |
| SEM-2033 | Topology boundary | A logical square processor grid does not imply a matching physical GPU/network topology or equal expand/fold costs. | Named device, fabric, communicator mapping, and route. | Note 189 |
| SEM-2034 | Distributed pull predicate | An unvisited vertex enters the next layer iff some exact incoming neighbor belongs to the closed global current frontier. | Directed orientation declared; undirected is the symmetric special case. | Notes 14 and 190 |
| SEM-2035 | Frontier-replication contract | Each bottom-up process needs exact local membership for every frontier predecessor it may inspect; full global replication is unnecessary under aligned 2D adjacency. | Explicit checkerboard and exact bitmap segments. | Beamer et al.; note 190 |
| SEM-2036 | Systolic protocol fact | The studied 2D bottom-up algorithm uses `p_c` substeps and rotates candidate completion responsibility along processor rows. | Beamer et al. Algorithm 4 layout. | Note 190 |
| SEM-2037 | Coverage invariant | An uncompleted candidate must visit every shard that can contain an incoming parent before a negative level result is final. | Complete incoming adjacency partition. | Note 190 |
| SEM-2038 | Witness invariant | A completion bit may suppress later shards only after an exact current-frontier predecessor is found. | One-parent/distance output. | Note 190 |
| SEM-2039 | Publication invariant | Candidate completion cannot safely outrun durable parent and next-frontier publication. | Discovery-to-publication continuity. | Notes 178 and 190 |
| SEM-2040 | Error-direction fact | False completed can lose reachability, while false uncompleted adds scans/proposals and requires exact duplicate handling. | Completion bit used as definitive skip state. | Note 190 |
| SEM-2041 | Latency boundary | Systolic early exit prevents later-shard work only at substep granularity and adds an `O(p_c)` substep critical path per bottom-up level in the source model. | Beamer et al. schedule; machine time unmeasured here. | Note 190 |
| SEM-2042 | Ordering boundary | Shard and neighbor order can change work and arbitrary parent without changing scalar distance under exact fixed-frontier semantics. | Complete coverage and valid same-level witnesses. | Note 190 |
| SEM-2043 | Rich-output boundary | First-hit completion is insufficient for canonical parent, complete predecessor DAG, exact path count, or canonical multi-source label. | Richer output requires later equal-depth contributions. | Notes 14, 57, and 190 |
| SEM-2044 | Traffic decomposition | Distributed bottom-up exchanges frontier bitmap segments, rotating completion state, and parent updates rather than top-down candidate streams alone. | Studied 2D protocol. | Beamer et al.; notes 189-190 |
| SEM-2045 | GPU reporting rule | Candidate shards, checks before/after hit, bitmap bytes, parent bytes, substep synchronization, and mode conversion are separate measurements. | Multi-GPU bottom-up probe. | Note 190 |
| SEM-2046 | Implicit-transfer boundary | Inverse generators do not supply the enumerable unvisited universe, exact frontier bitmap, or candidate responsibility state required by systolic pull. | Implicit/Cayley graph. | Notes 14 and 190 |
| SEM-2047 | Level-closure rule | A bottom-up level closes only after frontier distribution, all responsibility rotations/scans, completion transfers, and parent/next-frontier publications close globally. | Distributed exact level-synchronous execution. | Notes 56, 174, and 190 |

## Representation and state-space claims

| ID | Status | Claim | Assumptions and scope | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| REP-01 | Proved fact | Explicit, implicit, and Cayley presentations share the same BFS recurrence when identity and successor enumeration describe the same graph. | Exact identity; complete unit-cost successor oracle. | Note 06 graph-oracle model |
| REP-02 | Inference | Dense-ID explicit graphs encourage state, key, rank, payload, and owner to collapse into one integer; this convenience does not transfer automatically. | Common CSR-style representation, not every explicit graph. | Notes 01/04/06 |
| REP-03 | Proved fact | A hash is not exact identity unless injectivity over the reachable domain is proved or collisions receive exact comparison. | Ordinary many-to-one hashing. | Collision counterexample and reference tests |
| REP-04 | Proved fact | A bijective dense rank is sufficient for exact indexing but not necessarily sufficient for successor generation. | Rank/unrank and moves may have different costs/information needs. | Note 06; local oracle carries permutation plus rank |
| REP-05 | Proved fact | Inverse moves alone do not make conventional pull BFS available. | Pull also needs cheap enumeration of the unvisited universe and frontier membership. | Notes 01/05/06 |
| REP-06 | Proved fact | Symmetry canonicalization changes vertex identity to equivalence classes unless the original problem already uses those classes. | Path and distance preservation require a quotient/lifting proof. | Notes 03/06 and quotient counterexample |
| REP-07 | Unknown | Which real target domains admit compact bijective rank/update operations cheaper than full-state equality and storage? | Domain-specific. | Open questions |

## Cayley-graph knowledge

| ID | Status | Claim | Assumptions and scope | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| CAY-01 | Proved fact | BFS from the identity computes generator word length. | Cayley edges apply one generator; unit cost. | Note 03/06 |
| CAY-02 | Proved fact | Changing the generator collection can change degree, metric, diameter, labels, and relation-induced duplicates. | Exact effect depends on the collection and whether labels/multiplicity are semantic. | Note 06 |
| CAY-03 | Proved fact | For permutations with all adjacent transpositions, distance from identity equals inversion count and diameter is `n(n-1)/2`. | Generators swap adjacent positions. | Lower bound plus bubble-sort construction in note 06 |
| CAY-04 | Validated fact | The local `S_8` adjacent-transposition traversal has 40,320 states, diameter 28, peak frontier 3,836, and 282,240 generated transitions. | Ordered seven-generator model in REF-002. | REF-002 and Rust exhaustive oracle |
| CAY-05 | Observation | In the same `S_8` model, most generated transitions do not discover new states. | This finite family and instrumentation semantics only. | REF-002/003 |
| CAY-06 | Validated fact | Adding an identity generator added 40,320 same-level hits; duplicating a generator added 40,320 batch-duplicate occurrences without changing unique candidates. | Exact REF-004 `S_8` configurations. | REF-004 |
| CAY-07 | Validated fact | Adding the tested 3-cycle pair reduced diameter from 28 to 22, broadened the peak from 3,836 to 4,420, and introduced same-level edges. | Exact REF-004 generator pair; not every extra generator. | REF-004 |
| CAY-08 | Unknown | How representative are symmetric-group adjacent-transposition relations of puzzle groups such as cube and Megaminx state spaces? | Requires several graph families and state encodings. | No current cross-family evidence |
| CAY-09 | Proved fact | A finite move collection gives finite directed reachable balls, even for an infinite group, but does not by itself supply decidable equality of word representatives. | Reaching the whole group also requires a symmetric/inverse-complete convention; effective traversal requires computable action and exact equality. | Note 09 and Novikov-Boone theorem |
| CAY-10 | Proved fact | Adjacent-transposition `S_n` frontier sizes are Mahonian inversion counts with palindromic q-factorial generating polynomial and diameter `n(n-1)/2`. | All adjacent swaps from the identity. | Note 10; exact `S_8` profile in REF-002/003 |
| CAY-11 | Validated fact | In the local `S_8` graph, `7*w_d = batch_duplicates_d + w_(d-1) + w_(d+1)` at every depth. | Seven involutive adjacent swaps; bipartite inversion-parity graph. | REF-003 raw table and derivation in note 10 |

## Search-schedule observations

| ID | Status | Claim | Assumptions and scope | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| SCH-01 | Validated fact | The local smaller-frontier bidirectional implementation preserved distance/path in its exhaustive small directed corpus and `S_4` pairs. | REF-007 implementation and enumerated corpus. | REF-007 |
| SCH-02 | Rejected universal | "Bidirectional BFS always reduces work exponentially." | Counterexamples include shallow targets, asymmetry, meeting overhead, and graph shape. | REF-007 depth sweep and note 05 |
| SCH-03 | Observation | Strict alternation, smaller-frontier, and edge-work policies coincided on regular symmetric `S_8`. | Equal fixed degree makes vertex and edge estimates proportional in this graph. | REF-009 |
| SCH-04 | Observation | A measured edge-work selector dominated simpler policies on the tiny irregular corpus. | Small synthetic corpus; selector overhead not represented as GPU timing. | REF-009 |
| SCH-05 | Inference | Side-selection sophistication is useful only when its saved expansion work exceeds reduction/synchronization overhead. | Requires workload and hardware-specific costs. | REF-009; not yet measured on GPUs |
| SCH-06 | Validated fact | Candidate-stop, layer-stop, and batch-stop can all return exact target distance under their modeled contracts while doing different amounts of work. | REF-008 CPU models. | REF-008 |

## Distribution and multi-GPU concepts

| ID | Status | Claim | Assumptions and scope | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| DIST-01 | Proved fact | With sharded visited state, exact membership needs one authoritative decision or an equivalent exact distributed protocol. | A deterministic owner is one realization, not the only possible protocol. | Note 04 |
| DIST-02 | Validated fact | The local owner-computes simulation preserved bidirectional exactness across its tested rank counts and ownership layouts. | CPU simulation, not a timed multi-GPU runtime. | REF-010 |
| DIST-03 | Rejected universal | "One tested partition rule optimizes balance, communication, duplicates, and capacity simultaneously." | 35 strategies in the REF-006 model form a Pareto set. | REF-006 |
| DIST-04 | Rejected universal | "Deferred parent metadata always reduces wire bytes." | It saved bytes in selected deep runs but lost on every tested depth-2 case. | REF-011 |
| DIST-05 | Unknown | Whether any simulated byte reduction yields elapsed-time speedup on real interconnects. | Needs topology, collectives, overlap, latency, and packing costs. | REF-010/011 explicitly do not measure this |
| DIST-06 | Conceptually formalized; runtime unknown | Global unfinished-key bounds and conserved logical obligations provide safety conditions for target termination with in-flight work. | A concrete protocol still needs consistent-cut and failure-injection validation; minimal synchronization cost is not established. | Notes 56, 173, and 174; note 177 audit |

## GPU observations: deliberately narrow scope

These entries describe retained probes.  They are not a mandate to continue
optimizing the implementation.

| ID | Status | Claim | Assumptions and scope | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| GPU-01 | Validated fact | Docker can build the Rust-host/CUDA-translation-unit boundary and execute a native `sm_86` smoke workload on the available RTX 3070 Laptop GPU. | REF-012 environment snapshot; hardware/software may drift. | REF-012 |
| GPU-02 | Observation | Warp equal-key aggregation greatly helped the co-located single-key synthetic workload and slightly regressed distributed-duplicate cases. | REF-014 exact dense 32-bit bitmap backend, tested GPU/configurations. | REF-014 |
| GPU-03 | Rejected universal | "Warp aggregation is always better for duplicate-heavy batches." | Global duplicate count does not imply equal keys meet within a warp. | REF-014 and REF-016 |
| GPU-04 | Rejected universal | "Block output compaction always beats one global append atomic." | The tested all-new workload was slower with block compaction. | REF-014 |
| GPU-05 | Rejected universal | "Global sort/unique is the best exact dense-rank visited strategy." | CUB pipeline lost to the best direct bitmap path in tested dense 32-bit profiles and used more memory. | REF-015 |
| GPU-06 | Observation | Parent-major `S_8` expansion exposed far more warp-local equal successors than generator-major expansion; frontier order alone also changed locality substantially. | REF-016 exact recorded batches. | REF-016 |
| GPU-07 | Inference | Frontier order is performance-relevant carried state even when exact frontier sets are identical. | Consequence observed for locality; end-to-end importance is workload-dependent. | REF-016/017 |
| GPU-08 | Observation | In the fused `S_9` probe, parent-major warp had a lower median and generator-major a higher median; host-observed traversal was roughly seven times the summed kernel intervals. | Warmed RTX 3070 Laptop measurements; clocks unlocked and ranges overlap; residual time does not isolate synchronization cost. | REF-017 |
| GPU-09 | Unknown | Whether REF-017's modest parent-major effect persists at saturated scale, on other GPUs, or in other Cayley graphs. | Needs broader evidence; not an implementation commitment. | REF-017 boundary |
| GPU-10 | Unknown | Which cost dominates a real application: generation, visited identity, frontier materialization, synchronization, communication, or capacity. | Cannot be inferred from isolated primitives. | No application-scale trace yet |
| GPU-11 | Proved fact | A benign race is relative to the promised output: one arbitrary previous-layer parent may preserve a shortest tree while failing deterministic-parent or all-shortest-path contracts. | Exact visited claim and correct level; capacity handled independently. | Note 07 and Graph500 output contract |
| GPU-12 | Inference | Constant generator count removes degree-count imbalance but not transformation cost, identity locality, legality, ranking, or duplicate-convergence imbalance. | Regular implicit/Cayley expansion. | Note 07; REF-016/017 cover identity locality only |
| GPU-13 | Definition | Strong scaling, weak scaling, and capacity scaling are distinct multi-GPU claims. | Every performance report must state which problem size is held fixed or grown. | Note 07 |
| GPU-14 | Proved fact | Local frontier emptiness cannot prove distributed termination while another rank has work or messages in flight. | Distributed execution with noninstantaneous communication. | Note 07 |

## Preserved failures and corrections

| ID | What went wrong | Why it matters | Current disposition |
|---|---|---|---|
| FAIL-01 | REF-005 and REF-009 each had a failed initial launch. | Orchestration failure is distinct from an algorithmic result. | Corrected commands retained in reports. |
| FAIL-02 | REF-012 initially used an incorrect Docker build path. | Host success would not satisfy the Docker-only execution contract. | Docker path corrected and native cubin smoke verified. |
| FAIL-03 | REF-014 initially had wrong Dockerfile/sanitizer entrypoint invocations. | A missing sanitizer run must not be described as clean. | Corrected Docker-only commands passed. |
| FAIL-04 | REF-016's first artifact hid a rank-sorted-frontier assumption. | An uncontrolled order could be mistaken for a graph property. | Replaced with controlled rank/discovery/shuffled variants. |
| FAIL-05 | REF-017's first timing sweep included cold start in one configuration. | It biased a configuration comparison. | Rejected and overwritten; final oracle/warmup/repeated results are retained, but the first raw sweep is not retained as a separate artifact. |

## Cross-cutting lessons that current evidence supports

1. **Correctness and work are separate axes.** Exact frontier sets can coexist
   with radically different candidate multiplicity, parent choice, locality,
   communication, and capacity pressure.
2. **Duplicate ratio is insufficient.** Location and stage of convergence—same
   parent, batch, warp, owner, earlier level—determine which mechanism can remove
   the work.
3. **Ordering is semantically optional but operationally real.** It can affect
   parents, cancellation work, locality, routing, and the next physical frontier.
4. **Representation assumptions dominate transferability.** Dense-ID explicit
   methods do not automatically apply to wide implicit states.
5. **No universal policy has survived the probes.** Partition, metadata
   protocol, aggregation, compaction, sorting, and side selection all have
   workload-dependent boundaries.
6. **Primitive speed is not traversal speed.** Level control, transfers,
   capacity, communication, and generation can dominate a fast isolated kernel.

## Highest-value unresolved gaps

These gaps are conceptual or evidential, not an automatic coding backlog:

1. Validate notes 56 and 173--174 against a concrete partially expanded
   distributed execution with delay, retry and failure injection; the semantic
   and logical-credit conditions are formalized, but no runtime protocol has
   been validated against them.
2. Apply notes 56-57 and note 59's transfer checklist to a real exact multi-GPU
   BFS runtime artifact; REF-010 is now audited but remains a one-process
   bulk-synchronous simulation.
3. Apply note 55's successor-validation ladder to an independently specified
   application-scale puzzle oracle; the strategy now exists, but large-domain
   independent coverage evidence does not.
4. Execute note 184's simultaneous 54-position and signed-label conjugacy
   check between the pinned CayleyPy and DeepCubeA actions; source inspection
   narrowed the contract, but REF-029 still did not execute the second runtime.
5. Measure, rather than redefine, the regimes separated in notes 165--166:
   fixed-work strong scaling, capacity/weak scaling, throughput, and latency.
6. Validate notes 175--176's canonical-output contracts across worker/GPU
   counts, keeping set, distance, parent, word, count, and connector closure as
   separate outputs.
7. Test notes 167--172's algebraic owner/quotient claims on a bounded exact
   coset-orbit fixture before asking whether they reduce real routing.

The coverage status and promotion rules are audited in note 177. Conceptual
coverage is not real-runtime evidence and does not authorize an optimizer.

| SEM-2049 | Proved fact | Deleting edges cannot reduce the original unit shortest-path distance to a retained vertex. | Same vertex identities and unit edge costs; unreachable is allowed. | Note 198 |
| SEM-2050 | Qualified fact | If every goal path has a retained representative that is a permutation of the same actions, unit-cost optimal goal distance is preserved. | Permutation preserves length; the reduction is an edge-deleted subgraph. | Xu et al. 2011 Theorem 1 and Corollary 1; note 198 |
| SEM-2051 | Rejected inference | Preservation of optimal distance to a goal set implies preservation of the full BFS distance map. | Independent two-bit actions retain the distance-one goal while an omitted depth-one intermediate state is lost. | Note 198 |
| SEM-2052 | Rejected inference | Pairwise action commutation alone authorizes pruning one of the actions. | A coverage/proviso condition is also required so relevant omitted paths have retained representatives. | Xu et al. 2011 Definitions 11-16; note 198 |
| SEM-2053 | Corrected output boundary | Goal-trace coverage may lose other intermediate states; equal-length same-endpoint coverage of every finite source path preserves every source distance and distinct-state frontier. | Both can lose shortest-word multiplicity and canonical choices; transition-occurrence work is not frontier cardinality. The earlier unqualified per-trace loss claim was too broad. | Direct shortest-path proof in corrected note 198; note 66 |

## Rule for future updates

Every new result should either update an existing claim with broader/narrower
scope or add a new claim with an explicit status.  A measurement may promote an
unknown to an observation; only a proof or exhaustive stated scope may promote
it to a fact.  One counterexample is sufficient to reject a universal claim,
but does not identify the best alternative.
